CORRECTION BATCH 001 — 2026-10-06
================================================================================

FIX 001 — CROSS-DOMAIN ZERO-RESULT FILTER DEAD-END
Audit finding:
- CDO-DEF-006 — Zero-result filter state hides filters and Clear filters recovery control.
- Severity in audit: High UX / navigation / evidence-discovery defect.

Problem before correction:
- When a legitimate Cross-Domain filter returned zero evidence, `hasEvidence` became false.
- The page then hid the entire filter section, including Clear filters.
- The empty state incorrectly said `No decision comparisons yet`, even though tenant evidence existed but was filtered out.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/CrossDomainOptimizationPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Added explicit `hasActiveEvidenceFilters` state derived from the business-area/status/objective/option/tradeoff/result filters.
- Filter controls now remain visible when an active filter returns zero matching evidence.
- Zero-result filtered state now says `No evidence matches the current filters` instead of claiming no comparisons exist.
- Zero-result filtered state now provides a direct `Clear filters` recovery button.
- The planning-run chooser remains shown only when matching evidence exists, avoiding an empty selector.
- Existing true first-use/no-data behavior remains unchanged when there are no active evidence filters.

Expected live retest:
1. Open Cross-Domain Optimization with existing evidence.
2. Apply a filter known to return zero evidence (previous reproduction used Business area = Procurement).
3. Confirm Filter the evidence remains visible.
4. Confirm active filter remains visible.
5. Confirm Clear filters remains visible.
6. Confirm empty-state text says no evidence matches current filters rather than no comparisons exist.
7. Click Clear filters and confirm existing evidence returns.

Verification completed locally:
- New targeted regression check: PASS.
- Existing Cross-Domain operational completion check v3.49.170: PASS.
- TypeScript syntax transpile check for changed TSX file: PASS.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 002 — TENANT AUDIT ACTION-CODE EXAMPLE WAS WRONG / MISLEADING
Audit findings:
- Dedicated Action code test used `shipment.receive`, based on the UI example, and returned 0.
- Broad Search audit history with `shipment.receive` found two Shipment Received rows because the actual stored action contains that substring.
- Audit recorded the dedicated filter result as an inconsistency/functional failure from the tenant UI perspective.
- Entity type testing also showed that exact technical/internal values are required (`shipments`, not visible singular `Shipment`).

Root cause confirmed in source:
- Tenant Audit dedicated Action code filter is an exact-match backend filter (`al.action = value`).
- Actual shipment-received action code is `shipment.received`.
- Frontend placeholder incorrectly instructed the user with `Example: shipment.receive`.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/TenantAuditPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Action code placeholder changed from `Example: shipment.receive` to `Exact code, e.g. shipment.received`.
- Entity type placeholder changed from `Example: shipments` to `Exact type, e.g. shipments`.
- This keeps exact-match semantics intact while no longer instructing users to enter a non-existent action code.

Expected live retest:
1. Administration -> Audit.
2. Reset filters.
3. Enter `shipment.received` in Action code.
4. Apply filters.
5. Confirm Shipment Received audit events are returned.
6. Confirm the field now clearly states an exact code is required.

Verification completed locally:
- New targeted regression check: PASS.
- Existing Tenant Audit page hardening check: PASS.
- TypeScript syntax transpile check for changed TSX file: PASS.

THEME — ACTION FEEDBACK CORRECTNESS + ADVANCED-INVENTORY DISCOVERABILITY

FIX 003 — MANUAL STOCK ADJUSTMENT SUCCESS TOAST WAS SEMANTICALLY WRONG
Audit finding:
- Stock positive/negative manual adjustment persisted correctly, but the global toast said `Stock created successfully.` while inline feedback correctly said `Stock adjustment applied successfully.`

Frontend file changed:
- src/lib/api.ts

Surgical correction:
- Exact POST `/stock/adjust` now produces `Stock adjustment applied successfully.`
- Exact POST `/stock/count` now produces `Stock count applied successfully.`
- Existing stock mutation behavior and backend contract are unchanged.

Expected live retest:
1. Perform one controlled manual stock adjustment.
2. Confirm only adjustment-specific success wording is shown.
3. Confirm quantity refresh and movement history remain unchanged.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 004 — DECISION-INTELLIGENCE REFRESH ACTIONS USED GENERIC `ITEM CREATED` TOASTS
Audit findings:
- Adaptive Policy Engine `Refresh policy analysis` showed `Item created successfully.`
- Probabilistic Forecasting `Refresh forecast analysis` showed `Item created successfully.`
- Audit explicitly classified both as misleading because these actions refresh/rebuild analysis evidence rather than create a generic tenant item.

Frontend files changed:
- src/lib/api.ts
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Surgical correction:
- `/decision-intelligence/adaptive-policy-engine-refresh` now reports `Policy analysis refreshed successfully.`
- `/decision-intelligence/probabilistic-forecasting-refresh` now reports `Forecast analysis refreshed successfully.`
- Added five-language tenant translation rows for both messages.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 005 — PROCUREMENT RECOMMENDATION DECISION TOAST WAS GENERIC
Audit finding:
- Approving a procurement recommendation succeeded but toast said `Item created successfully.` even though no generic item was created.

Frontend files changed:
- src/lib/api.ts
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Surgical correction:
- Recommendation decision POST now reads the submitted decision status and returns action-specific success copy:
  - Recommendation approved successfully.
  - Recommendation rejected successfully.
  - Recommendation deferred successfully.
- Bulk-decision equivalents are also covered.
- Added five-language translations.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 006 — REQUISITION WORKFLOW/FULFILLMENT TOASTS WERE GENERIC; DUPLICATE TOAST NOISE SAFEGUARD
Audit findings:
- Requisition fulfillment succeeded but showed three identical `Item created successfully.` toasts.
- Audit requested one workflow-specific success message such as `Requisition fulfilled successfully.`

Frontend files changed:
- src/lib/api.ts
- src/app/AppProviders.tsx
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Surgical correction:
- Requisition fulfillment now reports `Requisition fulfilled successfully.`
- Bulk fulfillment reports `Requisitions fulfilled successfully.`
- Submit / approve / reject / cancel / reopen now use workflow-specific success messages.
- Added five-language translations.
- Added a narrow global toast dedupe guard: only near-simultaneous byte-identical feedback (same surface/type/message/request ID within 1.2 seconds) is collapsed. This prevents one operation from rendering duplicate identical success toasts while leaving different messages untouched.

Expected live retest:
1. Use a safe requisition workflow transition or controlled fulfillment.
2. Confirm one action-specific success toast appears.
3. Confirm no duplicate identical toast stack appears.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

CORRECTION BATCH 003 — 2026-10-06
================================================================================

THEME — NAVIGATION STATE DURABILITY + LONG-PAGE ACTION FOCUS + GLOBAL SHELL SAFETY

FIX 011 — OPERATIONS FEED FILTER/SEARCH STATE WAS TRANSIENT ACROSS BACK/F5
Audit findings:
- OPER-DEF-005 — Operations Feed navigation state is lost on browser Back.
- OPER-DEF-006 — Operations Feed filters/search do not survive hard refresh.
- Proven audit scope included Work area, Urgency, Search current feed, Time, and View.

Current-source review before correction:
- Work area and Urgency were read from URL only during first render, then copied into component state.
- Search, Time, and View were component-only state with no URL persistence.
- User control changes did not update the route, so Back/F5 could not reconstruct the working view.
- The separate older OPER-DEF-004 alert deep-link finding is already corrected in the current source: alert source links include `alert_id` when the source UUID is available. No new patch was applied for OPER-DEF-004 in this batch.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/RealTimeOperationsFeedPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- All five user controls are now route-backed:
  - `event_domain`
  - `urgency`
  - `search`
  - `time`
  - `view`
- Controls hydrate directly from URL search parameters on render/remount.
- Control changes update URL search parameters with `replace: true`, preserving durable/shareable state without creating a browser-history entry for every keystroke/filter change.
- Existing `timeline_item_id` deep-link context is preserved when filter parameters change.
- Invalid/unavailable work-area values are removed from the URL instead of leaving a stale inaccessible filter.

Expected live retest:
1. Open Operations Feed.
2. Set Work area = Execution tasks, Urgency = Medium, Search = product.
3. Confirm the URL now contains the active filter state.
4. Press F5 and confirm all active controls and the same result set remain.
5. Open a source workflow and use browser Back; confirm the prior Operations Feed controls are restored.
6. Separately set Time = Today and View = New since last visit, refresh, and confirm both survive.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 012 — LEARNING FEEDBACK VIEW DETAILS / EDIT OPENED CONTENT OUTSIDE VIEWPORT
Audit findings:
- LF-DEF-001 — View details opens the detail panel outside the current viewport.
- LF-DEF-002 — Edit opens the edit form outside the current viewport.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/DecisionLearningFeedbackPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Added dedicated focus/scroll targets for the Feedback evidence details panel and the Record what happened edit form.
- After a detail record finishes loading, the rendered detail container scrolls into view and receives programmatic focus.
- After an existing record enters edit mode, the existing feedback form scrolls into view and receives programmatic focus.
- Replaced the old broad `window.scrollTo({ top: 0 })` edit workaround with exact component targeting.
- Added `scrollMarginTop` to the targets so the focused section is not jammed against surrounding shell content.

Expected live retest:
1. Learning Feedback -> Policy effectiveness -> View details.
2. Confirm Feedback evidence details becomes visible immediately without manual scrolling.
3. Close details.
4. Click Edit on the same row.
5. Confirm Record what happened/edit form becomes visible immediately with the existing record loaded.

- src/layouts/AppLayout.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Changed the tenant `<main>` content flex behavior from shrinkable `flex: 1` to `flex: 1 0 auto`, so long page content owns its required height instead of being compressed into the footer region.
- Increased the normal bottom content safety gap before the footer:
  - desktop bottom padding: 40px
  - mobile bottom padding: 32px
- Footer remains in normal document flow; no page-specific footer patches were added.

Expected live retest:

- Review score was displayed with the generic fraction formatter even though the backend review score is already an integer percentage on a 0–100 scale.

- src/pages/CrossDomainOptimizationPage.tsx

- Review cards now render the backend score directly as a percentage.
- Existing 0–1 ratio/score fields continue using the original percentage formatter.

Result:

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 019 — CDO-DEF-003 — SELECTED CROSS-DOMAIN PLANNING RUN LOST ON BACK / F5
Audit finding:
- The selected Cross-Domain planning run existed only in component state and was lost on browser Back, refresh, or remount.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/CrossDomainOptimizationPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Selected run is now backed by the `review_run_id` URL query parameter.
- Opening a run updates `review_run_id` using replace semantics.
- Page initialization and browser URL changes restore the selected run from the query parameter.
- Clearing/changing evidence filters also clears the selected-run URL state intentionally.

Result:
- Selected planning-run context is durable across refresh and browser navigation.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 020 — CDO-DEF-004 — LEARNING FEEDBACK HANDOFF LOST EXACT RUN / OPTION CONTEXT
Audit finding:
- Cross-Domain opened Learning Feedback generically, forcing the user to rediscover the exact planning run/option.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/CrossDomainOptimizationPage.tsx
- src/pages/DecisionLearningFeedbackPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Cross-Domain now opens Learning Feedback with durable query context:
  - mode=optimization-results
  - source_id=<selected Cross-Domain run id>
  - option_id=<selected option id> when available
- Learning Feedback now consumes these query parameters.
- After source data loads, it selects the matching optimization-results source and matching option when present, including the expected-evidence snapshot used by the existing form.
- Existing manual navigation remains supported when no handoff query is supplied.

Result:
- The exact Cross-Domain run and selected option carry into Learning Feedback instead of landing on a generic page state.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 021 — CDO-DEF-005 — INTELLIGENCE REVIEW HANDOFF LOST EXACT REVIEW CONTEXT
Audit finding:
- Cross-Domain opened Intelligence Review generically instead of identifying the exact selected optimization run/review action.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/CrossDomainOptimizationPage.tsx

Backend contract verified, no backend edit required:
- Cross-Domain run detail already exposes an `intelligence_review` handoff with `source_action_id`.

Surgical correction:
- Run-detail handoff typing now carries `source_action_id`.
- Cross-Domain builds `/intelligence-review?source_action_id=...` from the backend handoff.
- A deterministic `optimization_run:<run id>` fallback is retained when the handoff does not supply the identifier.
- Both existing Intelligence Review entry actions now use the contextual path.

Result:
- Intelligence Review receives exact source-action context for the selected Cross-Domain run.

Date: 6 Oct 2026
================================================================================

AUTHORITATIVE INPUTS / BASELINE
- Frontend cumulative working baseline: surgical-fix-004, originally derived from `hotel-inventory-frontend(7).zip`.
- Backend baseline: authoritative `hotel-inventory-backend(7).zip` (Batches 001-004 required no backend source changes).

FIX 022 — MOBILE-DEF-003 — MOBILE EXECUTION RESPONSIBILITY / FILTER STATE LOST ON REFRESH
Audit finding:
- Mobile Execution responsibility scope and local filters were component-only state, so F5 / Back / bookmarked navigation could lose the user's working queue context.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/MobileExecutionPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Responsibility scope, urgency filter, source filter and page are now URL-backed query state.
- Query parameters used: `scope`, `urgency`, `source`, `page`.
- Defaults remain My tasks / all urgencies / all sources / first page.
- Changing responsibility/filter scope resets pagination and updates the URL with replace semantics.
- Pagination also updates the URL.

Result:
- The current mobile queue context survives F5, browser Back/Forward and bookmarked/deep-linked navigation instead of silently reverting.

Historical guard update:
- scripts/check-mobile-execution-operational-completion-v349160.mjs now verifies the URL-backed default semantics instead of the obsolete in-memory `useState` implementation.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 023 — OPER-DEF-001 — OPERATIONS FEED FRESHNESS TIME DID NOT ADVANCE AFTER SUCCESSFUL AUTO-REFRESH
Audit finding:
- The visible "Feed updated" time could remain unchanged after a successful periodic refresh when the backend snapshot's generated timestamp did not change, making live refresh look stale/broken.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/RealTimeOperationsFeedPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Visible freshness now uses React Query's `dataUpdatedAt`, i.e. the time of the latest successful query refresh.
- Existing approximately one-minute auto-refresh, focus refresh and reconnect refresh behavior is unchanged.
- No claim is made that business data changed; the timestamp now truthfully states when this client last refreshed successfully.

Result:
- The visible freshness indicator advances after successful auto-refresh even when the underlying event snapshot is unchanged.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 024 — WFC-DEF-001 — WORKFLOW COMPOSER SUGGESTED AN OWNER STEP WHEN RESPONSIBILITY ALREADY EXISTED
Audit finding:
- Suggested steps could tell the user to "Choose the person responsible" even when the workflow already had an assignee/responsible role.

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/WorkflowAutomationComposerPage.tsx

Surgical correction:
- Added responsibility-state detection based on assignee, responsible role and assignment state.
- The `assign_human_owner` suggested step is filtered out whenever responsibility is already present.

Result:
- Suggested next steps now reflect the workflow's actual responsibility state instead of repeating an already-completed action.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 025 — PF-DEF-001 — PROBABILISTIC FORECASTING MONITORING-READINESS HEADLINE CONTRADICTED EVIDENCE
Audit finding:
- The monitoring decision could state that monitoring was blocked until model scope/outcome feed was ready even when scope and outcome evidence already existed and the actual blocker was confidence drift or another readiness gap.

Backend file changed:
- src/services/decisionIntelligence/probabilisticForecastingService.js

Frontend file changed:
- src/pages/ProbabilisticForecastingPage.tsx

Translation file changed:
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Surgical correction:
- Preserved `forecast_monitoring_sla_blocked_until_scope_and_outcome_feed_ready` only for actual missing model scope or observed-outcome evidence.
- Added specific backend decisions for:
  - confidence drift
  - replacement readiness
  - high-risk outcome gap
  - approved-model outcome gap
  - unresolved review gaps
- Added corresponding truthful tenant-facing labels and five-language catalog coverage.

Result:
- Monitoring-readiness wording now names the real evidence blocker instead of claiming missing scope/outcomes when those already exist.

FIX 026 — CDO-DEF-002 — CROSS-DOMAIN -> REPLENISHMENT PLANNING LOST SOURCE DECISION CONTEXT
Audit finding:
- Cross-Domain Optimization opened Replenishment Planning generically, forcing the user to rediscover the relevant run/line and risking an unrelated run being shown as if it were the source decision.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/CrossDomainOptimizationPage.tsx
- src/pages/ReplenishmentPlanningPage.tsx
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Backend files changed:
- src/validations/locationReplenishmentPlanning.validation.js
- src/services/procurement/locationReplenishmentPlanningService.js

Safe contract decision:
- The source data does NOT provide a trustworthy persisted Replenishment Planning run ID for the original Cross-Domain recommendation.
- The fix therefore does NOT invent/fabricate a run ID.
- Cross-Domain now carries durable source business context via `source_par_level_id`, plus source optimization plan/item IDs where available.
- Replenishment Planning asks the backend for saved runs matching that source par-level's exact product/location business scope.
- A matching purchase or destination-transfer line is visibly marked `Source decision match` and scrolled into view.
- If no matching saved run exists, the page explicitly states that no saved run matches this source decision and does NOT silently select an unrelated run.
- A "Show all planning runs" action lets the user deliberately leave the source-scoped view.
- Existing saved Cross-Domain options remain compatible through the prior source-id fallback when needed.

Backend behavior:
- GET planning-runs query accepts optional `source_par_level_id`.
- The service resolves that par level to product/storage-location scope.
- Only saved planning runs containing the matching purchase or destination-transfer scope are returned for that handoff.
- Handoff product/location IDs are returned to support exact-line highlighting.

Result:
- CDO-DEF-002 is now closed without pretending an exact historical run ID exists when the data contract cannot prove one.
- The user either lands on the correct saved run/line by business scope or receives an explicit no-match explanation.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 007 — DASHBOARD / ALERTS / ACTION CENTER SEMANTIC CLOSURE

FIX 037 — DASH-DEF-008 — DASHBOARD REORDER QUEUE COULD DISAGREE WITH PROCUREMENT RECOMMENDATIONS
File changed:
- frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx

Source-level cause identified:
- Dashboard queried `/reorder-insights/recommendations` without the `active_only=true` scope.
- Procurement Recommendations intentionally queries the same backend contract with `active_only=true`.
- The backend defines an active recommendation as a positive reorder recommendation that has not already been converted to a Purchase Order.
- Therefore Dashboard could promote a recommendation that the Procurement Recommendations active work queue intentionally excluded.

Correction:
- Dashboard reorder recommendations now request `active_only=true` while retaining the existing 30-day lookback, reorder-quantity sort, and six-card limit.

Result:
- Dashboard and Procurement Recommendations now use the same active-work semantics for reorder recommendations.
- This removes the confirmed code-path inconsistency that allowed a Dashboard reorder card to be absent from the Procurement Recommendations active queue.

Historical-data caution:
- The audit did not prove that the specific historical Crasssh Peanuts record had already been converted to a PO, so that historical explanation is still not asserted as fact.
- The source mismatch itself is now corrected; live retest should confirm the historical symptom no longer reproduces with current data.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Missing `Inside range` and Calibration values in actual-outcome rows now say `Not yet assessed`.
- Existing valid zero/false values remain distinct from unavailable values.

Result:
- Users can distinguish a real zero/No result from a value that cannot yet be assessed because evidence is incomplete.

FIX 082 — ADMINISTRATION / AUDIT — SEARCH DID NOT MATCH THE HUMAN LABEL SHOWN IN THE EVENT TABLE
Files changed:
- backend/src/services/tenancy/tenantAuditService.js
- backend/tests/tenant-audit-page-hardening.test.js

Audit evidence:
- `Search audit history = Custom Role Create` returned 0 even though that exact human-readable operation label was visible in the table.
- `Custom Role Created` also returned 0, demonstrating that punctuation-normalized action names and normal lifecycle wording were not discoverable.

Correction:
- Broad audit search now also compares punctuation-normalized action/entity values (`custom_role.create` -> `custom role create`).
- Added safe lifecycle-word normalization for common user wording such as Created -> create, Received -> receive, Deleted -> delete, Finalized -> finalize, etc.
- Search values remain parameterized; user text is not interpolated into SQL.

Result:
- The broad search can now find audit events using the wording users actually see in the event table.
- Natural lifecycle variants such as `Custom Role Created` can resolve to the stored `custom_role.create` action without weakening tenant isolation.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 083 — ADMINISTRATION / AUDIT — ACTION CODE / ENTITY TYPE REQUIRED USERS TO GUESS INTERNAL VALUES
Files changed:
- backend/src/services/tenancy/tenantAuditService.js
- backend/src/routes/audit.js
- frontend/src/pages/TenantAuditPage.tsx
- frontend/src/pages/TenantAuditPage.css
- frontend/src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Audit evidence:
- Dedicated Action code and Entity type fields were too technical for normal tenant users.
- `Shipment` returned 0 while the hidden exact entity value `shipments` returned 45 results.
- The audit explicitly recommended human-readable searchable/selectable values instead of requiring backend-code knowledge.

Correction:
- Added tenant-scoped `GET /audit/filter-options`.
- Backend returns distinct exact actions/entity types from the current tenant's own audit evidence only.
- Frontend now presents human-readable Action and Entity selectors while submitting the exact stored value behind the scenes.
- If the options endpoint is unavailable, the existing exact-code text input remains as a safe fallback.

Result:
- Users can choose recognizable business labels instead of guessing `shipment.received`, `shipments`, or similar internal values.
- Exact backend filter semantics remain unchanged and tenant-scoped.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 084 — ADMINISTRATION / AUDIT — PENDING VS APPLIED FILTER STATE WAS NOT CLEAR ENOUGH
Files changed:
- frontend/src/pages/TenantAuditPage.tsx
- frontend/src/pages/TenantAuditPage.css
- frontend/src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Audit evidence:
- The separate Apply filters step created friction and made it difficult to know whether edits were already active.
- CSV behavior intentionally uses applied filters rather than unfinished edits, so silently removing the apply boundary would change a tested and documented contract.

Correction:
- Kept the explicit Apply filters contract.
- Added a visible `Pending filter changes` state whenever draft filters differ from applied filters.
- Added an `Applied filters are active` state after filters are applied.

Result:
- Users can immediately tell whether the table/CSV is using the values currently typed in the filter form.
- The already-tested pending-vs-applied CSV behavior remains intact.

- Mobile width/top positioning is handled separately.
- Existing semantic success/error message content and accessibility roles are preserved.

Result:
- Completion/error feedback remains visible where the user is working instead of being hidden above a long permission catalog.

FIX 087 — MOBILE-UX-003 — MOBILE EXECUTION CONTROLS DID NOT VISUALLY DISTINGUISH RESPONSIBILITY, FILTERS, AND ACTIONS
Files changed:
- frontend/src/pages/MobileExecutionPage.tsx
- frontend/src/pages/MobileExecutionPage.css
- frontend/src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Audit evidence:
- Responsibility tabs, urgency/source dropdowns, Refresh, Sync, and Open Execution Tasks were presented with very similar outlined styling.
- The page did not make it obvious which controls changed queue scope/filtering versus which controls performed an action or opened another workflow.

Correction:
- Added a visible Responsibility group heading above My / Unassigned / Team task scope controls.
- Added visible Urgency and Source field labels around their dropdowns.
- Placed queue actions in a separate action row divided from filters.
- Strengthened dropdown field styling so selectors read as form controls rather than buttons.
- Strengthened the active responsibility scope state.
- Made Open execution tasks the clear primary navigation action.

Result:
- Responsibility selection, filters, and actions are visually separated and easier to understand without guessing control type.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 088 — MOBILE-UX-001 — `SYNC PENDING (0)` LOOKED LIKE AN AVAILABLE ACTION WHEN NOTHING WAS QUEUED
Files changed:
- frontend/src/pages/MobileExecutionPage.tsx
- frontend/src/pages/MobileExecutionPage.css
- frontend/src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Audit evidence:
- The queue summary showed zero pending actions, but `Sync pending (0)` was still presented in the control bar like an action.
- The control was disabled in source, but its visual presence still suggested that the user should do something.

Correction:
- The Sync button is now shown only when one or more offline actions are actually queued.
- When the queue is empty, the action is replaced by a neutral `No pending sync` status indicator.

Result:
- Zero pending work is communicated as state, not as a disabled action the user may think they need to click.

FIX 089 — MOBILE-UX-004 — MANUAL TASKS USED MISLEADING `OPEN SOURCE WORKFLOW` WORDING

- A Manual execution task has no separate source workflow.
- The previous button sent the user back to Execution Tasks while calling that destination `Open source workflow`.

Correction:
- Manual tasks now use the label `Open task details`.
- Manual-task navigation now includes the exact `task_id`, so Execution Tasks can open the exact task rather than only the generic task page.
- Non-manual source types keep `Open source workflow` and their existing exact source-record handoff behavior.

Result:
- The wording now matches the actual destination and Manual tasks open the exact task record.

FIX 090 — MOBILE-UX-005 — `TAKE PHOTO` WAS MISLEADING ON DESKTOP

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 017 — LEARNING FEEDBACK BUSINESS CLARITY / SCORE SEMANTICS
Date: 2026-10-06
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-016 + Backend surgical-fix-014
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the confirmed tenant-audit correction sequence without changing backend contracts.
- Focused on unresolved Learning Feedback business-language and score-interpretation findings.
- Backend semantics were inspected before changing presentation. Outcome, policy-effectiveness and optimization-value scores are normalized -1..1 values, while forecast error and the aggregate learning signal use different scales. The correction therefore makes the units explicit instead of incorrectly converting every metric to a percentage.

FIX 057 — LF-UX-001 — Technical learning posture wording dominated the tenant page
Files changed:
- src/pages/DecisionLearningFeedbackPage.tsx
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Correction:

- Other system posture values now map to concise tenant-facing states such as `Review required`, `Evidence monitored`, `No learning evidence yet`, `High drift pressure`, and `Review pressure present`.
- Replaced the prominent `Posture` / continuous-learning-posture presentation with the clearer label `Learning status`.
- Added German, Spanish, French and Croatian translations for the new tenant-visible wording.

Result:
- Internal governance posture codes remain available in source semantics, but ordinary tenant presentation now starts with understandable business language.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FIX 058 — LF-UX-002 / LF-UX-003 / LF-UX-007 / LF-UX-009 — Mixed score scales, overconfident zero/one-record signal and raw four-decimal scores
Files changed:
- src/pages/DecisionLearningFeedbackPage.tsx
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Correction:
- Added metric-specific labels so the UI no longer uses the ambiguous generic `Score / Error` label.
- Outcome evidence is labelled `Outcome score (-1 to 1)`.
- Policy evidence is labelled `Effectiveness score (-1 to 1)`.
- Optimization evidence is labelled `Value score (-1 to 1)`.
- Forecast evidence is labelled `Forecast error (%)` and consistently displays percentage error in the summary, evidence table and detail view.
- Normalized -1..1 scores are displayed with two decimals rather than raw four-decimal values such as `1.0000`.
- Learning signal and drift pressure are displayed as percentages because those aggregate values are actually 0–100 metrics.
- When there is zero evidence, Learning signal and Drift pressure display `—` rather than presenting the backend's neutral/default aggregate as a meaningful conclusion.
- Zero-evidence state now says `No learning signal yet — record evidence first.`
- A one-record evidence base now explicitly says `Preliminary signal — based on one evidence record.`
- Average metric labels in Learning Impact Assessment now state their units/scales explicitly.

Result:
- Users no longer have to infer whether 1, 1.0000 and 100 mean the same thing.
- The page avoids presenting a statistically strong-looking learning conclusion when the evidence base is empty or consists of only one record.

APE-UX-005 — `Refresh policy analysis` understated a persisted evidence-producing action
Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/AdaptivePolicyEnginePage.tsx
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Backend file changed:
- src/services/decisionIntelligence/adaptivePolicyEngineService.js

Correction:
- Renamed the governed action from `Refresh policy analysis` to `Run new policy analysis`.
- Pending copy is now `Running analysis…` rather than refresh wording.
- Added visible pre-action guidance explaining that running the analysis records a new evidence snapshot from current operating data and does not automatically apply policy changes.
- Empty-state recovery now says the action creates a fresh evidence snapshot instead of implying a passive rebuild/refresh.
- Backend lifecycle/readiness guidance that previously said `Refresh policy analysis` now consistently says `Run a new policy analysis` / `Run a new analysis` where the action records new evidence.
- The backend action itself, persistence model, permissions, calculations, recommendation behavior and policy-application boundaries were not changed.

Verified backend semantics:
- `tenant_decision_adaptive_policy_refresh_runs` records the analysis run.
- `tenant_decision_adaptive_policy_signals` receives append-only signal snapshots.
- `tenant_decision_adaptive_policy_effectiveness` receives analysis-run-bound observation/effectiveness snapshots.
- Backend response still reports `autonomous_application: false` and `operating_policy_mutated: false`.

Result:
- A normal tenant user is no longer told that a history-producing analysis run is merely a passive refresh.
- The UI discloses the durable evidence consequence before the user runs it.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 020 — LOW-STOCK SCOPE CLARITY ACROSS DASHBOARD / STOCK / REPORTS
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-019 + Backend surgical-fix-019
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the confirmed tenant-audit correction sequence from the cumulative source.
- Focused on CROSS-UX-002: the same generic `Low Stock` wording was used for legitimately different threshold scopes.
- Inspected the actual current frontend and backend threshold contracts before changing labels.
- Confirmed that the figures are not supposed to be forced to match:
  - Dashboard / Stock operational low-stock rows are product-location positions evaluated against an effective location threshold.
  - The effective location threshold is active location par level first, then an explicitly configured stock.min_quantity, otherwise zero/no location warning.
  - The Reports overview low-stock count calls the report with its default product scope, comparing tenant-wide product stock against products.min_stock across all locations.
  - The dedicated low-stock report can deliberately combine product-minimum rows and active location-par rows using its existing Threshold scope filter.

CROSS-UX-002 — `Low Stock` wording hid different threshold scopes
Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx
- src/pages/StockPage.tsx
- src/pages/InsightsPage.tsx
- src/pages/ReportsPage.tsx
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Correction — Dashboard / Stock / Insights location-scoped surfaces:
- Dashboard `Low Stock Rows` is now `Positions Below Location Minimum`.
- Dashboard/Stock helper wording now explains that these are product/location positions below an active location par level or an explicit stock minimum.
- Dashboard low-stock worklist uses the same location-scope wording instead of generic `Low Stock`.
- Dashboard row metric `Minimum` is now `Location Minimum` in this context.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 022 — DIGITAL TWIN CONNECTED-CONTEXT FOCUS CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-021 + Backend surgical-fix-019
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the confirmed tenant-audit correction sequence from the cumulative source.
- Skipped CDO-DEF-006 because it was already corrected in Batch 001.
- Took the next confirmed unresolved finding in audit order: Digital Twin `Show connections` produced no selected-context change for the audited tenant admin.
- Inspected the current Batch 021 frontend and cumulative Backend 019 endpoint contract before editing.

DIGITAL TWIN — `SHOW CONNECTIONS` NO-OP FOR DIAGNOSTICS-CAPABLE USERS
Canonical audit status:
- CONFIRMED DEFECT / functional navigation-state failure.
- The v93 ledger does not assign this finding a numbered Digital Twin defect ID.

Root cause confirmed in source:
- The backend deliberately has two safe response shapes:
  - ordinary/public tenant response maps a topology focus key to `node_key` and edge/overlay focus keys to `source_node_key` / `target_node_key`;
  - diagnostics-capable tenant readers retain the fuller diagnostics response, where those same safe public focus keys are named `public_node_key`, `source_public_node_key`, and `target_public_node_key`.
- The Digital Twin frontend only read the ordinary/public field names.
- For the audited tenant Admin with diagnostics access, topology cards therefore had a valid `public_node_key` but the click handler checked only `node.node_key`.
- The guard expression `node.node_key && setFocusNodeKey(node.node_key)` could therefore become a silent no-op.
- Dependency and overlay connected-context actions had the same response-shape mismatch risk.
- The review-first context path can still work because its backend response already exposes `review_first.node_key`, which explains the apparently inconsistent live behavior recorded in the audit.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/DigitalTwinVisualizationPage.tsx
- src/pages/DigitalTwinVisualizationPage.css
- scripts/check-digital-twin-operational-completion-v349175.mjs
- scripts/check-digital-twin-focus-surgical-fix-v349267.mjs (new)
- package.json

Surgical correction:
- Added response-shape-safe focus-key resolution:
  - topology point: `node_key` OR `public_node_key`;
  - dependency source: `source_node_key` OR `source_public_node_key`;
  - dependency target: `target_node_key` OR `target_public_node_key`;
  - overlay target/source: public-response key OR diagnostics-response public key.
- Topology-card selected styling uses the normalized focus key.
- `Show connections` uses the normalized key and is disabled when no safe key exists instead of silently accepting a no-op click.
- Dependency `Review source` / `Review affected record` and overlay `Review connected context` now use the same dual-shape-safe focus-key resolution.
- Added an exact Connected context panel ref.
- When the requested focus response resolves successfully for the selected key, the Connected context panel scrolls into view and receives programmatic focus.
- Added `scroll-margin-top` and visible focus treatment so the shell/header does not obscure the newly opened context.

Important non-change:
- Backend response behavior was deliberately not flattened or weakened.
- Diagnostics-capable readers still receive the existing full diagnostics response.
- Ordinary readers still receive the existing sanitized response with safe `dt_...` public focus keys.
- Backend focus validation remains restricted to the safe public `dt_[a-f0-9]{24}` key form.
- No raw graph IDs, tenant IDs, hidden source IDs, or other diagnostics identifiers were newly exposed to ordinary users.
- No Digital Twin graph-building, ranking, search, filters, source permissions, tenant isolation, operational-data mutation, Action Center behavior, or source-record routing logic changed.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 024 — SYSTEM CONTEXT SNAPSHOT JSONB PERSISTENCE CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-023 + Backend surgical-fix-019
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the confirmed tenant-audit correction sequence from the cumulative source.
- Took the next confirmed unresolved audit finding after the Batch 023 Reliability handoff closure.
- Finding: System Context -> Planning History -> Save context snapshot failed in production with `The request contains an invalid value format` and no snapshot was created.
- Inspected the current Frontend 023 request, Backend 019 validation/controller/orchestrator/service, PostgreSQL schema, and node-postgres parameter conversion before editing.

SYSTEM CONTEXT -> PLANNING HISTORY -> SAVE CONTEXT SNAPSHOT FAILURE
Canonical audit status:
- FUNCTIONAL FAIL / production defect confirmed.
- Save context snapshot exposed no editable user value that could explain/correct the failure.
- The failed snapshot also blocked downstream snapshot comparison and scenario-planning history flows that depend on saved history.

Root cause confirmed in source/runtime serialization contract:
- Frontend request is valid: POST `/system-context/snapshots/capture` with optional `sections` array.
- Backend Joi validation explicitly accepts the optional sections array.
- The controller correctly rebuilds a tenant-scoped read-only System Context snapshot before persistence.
- Snapshot storage uses PostgreSQL JSONB columns for:
  - `context_snapshot`;
  - `recommendation_summary`;
  - `predictive_readiness_summary`;
  - `forecast_scenarios`;
  - `historical_signal_window`.
- `forecast_scenarios` is a JavaScript array.
- node-postgres serializes JavaScript arrays as PostgreSQL array literals rather than JSON text when passed directly as query parameters.
- Directly verified in the supplied runtime:
  - array parameter prepares as PostgreSQL-array syntax `{...}`;
  - explicit `JSON.stringify(array)` prepares as valid JSON array text `[...]`.
- PostgreSQL JSONB rejects the PostgreSQL-array-form parameter with error code `22P02`.
- The existing global error handler maps `22P02` exactly to the production message `The request contains an invalid value format`.
- Therefore the observed production failure is explained by the JSONB parameter serialization boundary, not by invalid user input.

Backend file changed:
- src/services/systemContextSnapshotsService.js

Surgical correction:
- Added one narrow JSONB parameter encoder:
  - non-null values -> `JSON.stringify(value)`;
  - null/undefined -> SQL `NULL`.
- All five JSONB insert parameters now use that encoder.
- This guarantees that `forecast_scenarios` reaches PostgreSQL as JSON array text instead of PostgreSQL array syntax.
- Objects remain valid JSON objects.
- Optional summary/history values continue to persist as SQL NULL when absent rather than being converted to JSON null.

Important non-change:
- Frontend snapshot request contract was not changed because it is already valid.
- Snapshot table/schema was not changed.
- Tenant scope was not changed.
- `SYSTEM_CONTEXT_READ` + Decision Intelligence governance permission boundaries were not changed.
- Snapshot content/calculations were not changed.
- No execution request, stock, shipment, supplier, workflow, automation, or other operational mutation behavior was added.
- Snapshot capture remains read-only historical evidence persistence.

- predictive readiness summary is valid JSON text;
- forecast-scenario array is encoded as JSON `[...]`, not left as a JavaScript/PostgreSQL array;
- historical signal window is valid JSON text;
- tenant/source/created-by parameters remain unchanged;
- absent optional JSONB fields remain SQL NULL.

STOCK TRANSFERS — REJECTION MESSAGE OUTSIDE CURRENT VIEWPORT
Canonical audit status:
- Over-available quantity detection: PASS.
- Backend hard rejection: PASS.
- Validation error visibility after rejection: UX DEFECT / IMPROVEMENT REQUIRED.
- User had to manually scroll upward after Create transfer draft to discover the correct validation message.

Root cause confirmed in current source:
- StockTransfersPage keeps mutation errors in one page-level `error` state.
- That error state is rendered directly below the workspace summary cards, above the long create/edit form.
- Create/update mutation `onError` only set `error` + clear `message`; no focus or scroll operation followed.
- `handleSubmit` client validation behaved the same way.
- Therefore a rejection from the lower submit action could be correct but remain outside the current viewport.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/StockTransfersPage.tsx
- scripts/check-stock-transfers-validation-focus-surgical-fix-v349269.mjs (new)
- package.json

Surgical correction:
- Added a dedicated `errorFeedbackRef` to the existing page-level error message.
- Added `focusErrorFeedback()` which:
  - smoothly scrolls the error into the current viewport;
  - centers it rather than jumping the whole page to the absolute top;
  - moves programmatic focus to the error after scrolling.
- Create Transfer Draft API errors now call the focus helper.
- Save Draft Changes API errors now call the focus helper.
- Client-side form validation errors also call the focus helper.
- Stale selected-draft/version guidance also calls the focus helper.
- Existing error presentation now carries:
  - `tabIndex=-1` for programmatic focus without adding a normal tab stop;
  - `role=alert`;
  - `aria-live=assertive`;
  - fixed-header `scrollMarginTop` protection.

Important non-change:
- Existing pre-submit source-availability calculation remains intact.
- Valid Create transfer draft flow remains unchanged.
- Backend source-stock / reserved-stock protection remains unchanged.
- No stock, transfer, reservation, permission, execution, cancellation, movement-ledger, filtering, export, or audit behavior changed.
- Backend surgical-fix-024 remains the cumulative backend baseline.

STORAGE LOCATIONS — STALE CROSS-WORKFLOW FEEDBACK
Canonical audit evidence:
- after a blocked Housekeeping Closet retirement, the retirement-block error remained visible when the user moved on to the unrelated Create Storage Location workflow;
- after Start Over in the CSV import workflow, an older Storage Location success message was still visible, reinforcing the same stale-feedback problem.

Root cause confirmed in current source:
- form success/error state was cleared only by selected form actions;
- typing a new form value did not clear prior create/update/retire feedback;
- the shared CSV import panel had no way to tell StorageLocationsPage that the user had started a different workflow.

Correction:
- added a shared transient-feedback cleanup helper for Storage Locations form + list-refresh feedback;
- editing Name or Storage Condition now clears stale prior-operation feedback;
- import interaction now calls a narrow optional parent callback so selecting/validating/resetting import work clears unrelated Storage Location form feedback;
- edit/cancel/retire workflow transitions also clean stale transient feedback before showing the new workflow state.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

STORAGE LOCATIONS — REFRESH COMPLETION FEEDBACK
Canonical audit evidence:
- Refresh Locations correctly reloaded persisted data, but the prior `Storage location updated successfully.` message remained visible;
- no distinct refresh-complete feedback was visible, making an unchanged refresh look ambiguous.

Root cause confirmed in current source:
- Refresh Locations called `locationsQuery.refetch()` directly;
- it did not own a refresh-specific success/error state and did not clear the previous mutation message.

Correction:
- Refresh Locations now uses a dedicated async handler;
- prior create/update/retire feedback is cleared when refresh starts;
- successful refresh shows `Storage locations refreshed.` next to the list workflow;
- failed refresh shows `Unable to refresh storage locations.` next to the list workflow;
- both new messages are present in all five tenant locales.

BULK CSV VALIDATION — FALSE GENERIC SUCCESS FEEDBACK

Root cause confirmed in current source:
- inventory-import preview is implemented as POST `/inventory-imports/preview`;
- the shared API layer emits mutation feedback for POST by default;
- previewInventoryImport did not opt out even though the import panel already owns richer valid/invalid feedback.

Correction:
- previewInventoryImport now sets `skipMutationFeedback: true`;
- validation therefore no longer emits the generic global mutation-success toast;
- shared CSV panel now models feedback tone explicitly:
  - file loaded / pre-validation guidance = neutral info;
  - all rows validated = success;
  - invalid rows found = warning;
  - commit success remains success;
- validation/parsing/commit rules themselves were not changed.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 027 — ADVANCED INVENTORY LOCATION HIERARCHY OPTIONAL PICK-SEQUENCE CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-026 + Backend surgical-fix-024
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the cumulative source.
- Skipped the already-corrected Advanced Inventory desktop capability-navigation finding from Batch 002.
- Took the next confirmed unresolved functional defect that is still present in Backend 024: valid Location hierarchy saves fail when the current frontend omits optional `pick_sequence`.
- Backend-only surgical correction; no frontend behavior was changed.

ADVANCED INVENTORY -> LOCATION HIERARCHY — VALID SAVE FAILED WITH INVALID VALUE FORMAT
Canonical audit evidence:
- A valid/no-op hierarchy save for Storage Test Location failed with `The request contains an invalid value format.`
- The frontend request legitimately omitted `pick_sequence` because the current hierarchy form does not edit that field.
- Audit/source inspection isolated the failure to PostgreSQL integer parameter serialization rather than user input or hierarchy business validation.

Root cause reconfirmed in cumulative Backend surgical-fix-024:
- `saveLocationHierarchy()` correctly used a boolean SQL guard to decide whether `pick_sequence` should mutate.
- However the paired integer parameter was always computed as:
  `body.pick_sequence === null || body.pick_sequence === '' ? null : Number(body.pick_sequence)`.
- When the field is omitted, `body.pick_sequence` is `undefined`, so `Number(undefined)` becomes JavaScript `NaN`.
- PostgreSQL still parses/casts the bound `$9::integer` parameter even though the CASE guard is false, so NaN causes SQLSTATE 22P02 / invalid input format before the existing value can be preserved.

Backend file changed:
- src/services/inventory/inventoryCapabilitiesService.js

Surgical correction:
- Added explicit `hasPickSequence` detection once before the UPDATE.
- Added `pickSequenceParam` construction:
  - field omitted -> bind SQL NULL;
  - explicit null/empty -> bind SQL NULL;
  - explicit numeric value -> bind Number(value).
- The existing SQL boolean guard remains authoritative for whether the column is updated.
- Therefore an omitted field now supplies a harmless typed NULL parameter while preserving the stored `pick_sequence` value.

Important non-change:
- No hierarchy validation was removed or weakened.
- Parent-container eligibility remains enforced.
- Warehouse top-level rules remain enforced.
- Hierarchy ordering remains enforced.
- Cycle prevention remains enforced.
- Leaf-has-children protection remains enforced.
- Optimistic version locking remains enforced.
- Explicit integer `pick_sequence` updates remain supported.
- Explicit null `pick_sequence` clearing remains supported.
- No database schema or migration change was required.
- No frontend source changed.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. omitted `pick_sequence` binds NULL, never NaN, and preserves the current stored sequence;
2. explicit integer `pick_sequence` is still bound and saved;
3. explicit null `pick_sequence` remains a valid clear operation.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 028 — ADVANCED INVENTORY BOM LOCATION READINESS / EXECUTION FEEDBACK CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-026 + Backend surgical-fix-027
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the cumulative source.
- Took the next confirmed unresolved Advanced Inventory finding after the Location hierarchy backend closure.
- Finding: BOM assembly/disassembly Location selection presented every active storage location identically even when the selected BOM could not be executed there with current stock, forcing the operator to choose blindly, confirm the stock-changing action, and only then receive the backend rejection.
- The same workflow still used the shared generic mutation toast for BOM execution even though the page-owned inline message was action-specific.
- Frontend-only surgical correction; backend BOM/stock safety logic remains unchanged and authoritative.

ADVANCED INVENTORY -> BOM & ASSEMBLIES — BLIND LOCATION SELECTION BEFORE STOCK-CHANGING CONFIRMATION
Canonical audit evidence:
- A controlled assembly attempt at Storage Vezica used a BOM requiring HRC-0004 Crasssh Peanuts 60g while that location had 0 available stock.
- The Location selector still offered Storage Vezica like every other active location and exposed no quantity/eligibility cue.
- User had to select the location, click Assemble stock, confirm the irreversible stock-changing action, and only then receive the correct backend rejection `Stock adjustment would result in negative stock`.
- Audit classified this as a UX defect: the operator was effectively choosing an assembly location blind.
- Later controlled positive-path testing proved the same BOM can execute successfully once sufficient component stock exists, so the correct correction is pre-execution availability guidance, not weakening the backend safeguard.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-026:
- `BomPanel` loaded active BOM definitions and active storage locations.
- The Location `<select>` rendered only each location name.
- No current stock-read query or readiness calculation existed in the BOM execution UI.
- `requestExecution()` went directly from selected BOM/location/quantity to the browser confirmation dialog.
- The backend correctly remained the first place that rejected an impossible stock-changing operation.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/InventoryCapabilitiesPage.tsx
- src/lib/api.ts
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts
- scripts/check-bom-location-readiness-surgical-fix-v349271.mjs (new)
- package.json

Surgical correction — stock readiness before confirmation:
- Added a narrow BOM stock-readiness query using the existing tenant `/stock` read endpoint.
- The query is enabled only for users who already satisfy the existing BOM execution permission gate.
- For Assembly:
  - readiness evaluates every component in the selected BOM;
  - required quantity is scaled by requested finished quantity / BOM output quantity;
  - configured waste percentage is included;
  - current unreserved stock uses `projected_free_quantity` when supplied by the authoritative stock read contract;
  - fallback uses `quantity - reserved_quantity` if projected free quantity is absent.
- For Disassembly:
  - readiness checks the selected BOM output product's current unreserved quantity at each location.
- Once BOM + quantity are known, Location options now expose one of:
  - `Stock check passed`;
  - `Insufficient stock`.
- When a selected location is known to be insufficient:
  - the page shows the limiting product(s), current available quantity, and required quantity;
  - the stock-changing action button is disabled;
  - `requestExecution()` refuses to open the confirmation dialog.
- While current stock is loading, execution waits and shows `Checking current unreserved stock before execution…`.
- If the stock-readiness request itself fails, the UI does not invent a result; it states that local precheck is unavailable and that the server will still validate stock before posting any movement.
- A passing frontend stock check explicitly states that it is only an early usability check and that server-side lot, serial, reservation, and other inventory safeguards still apply at submission.

Important safety boundary:
- Backend BOM execution code was NOT changed.
- Backend continues to call the authoritative `stockService.adjustStock` for every component/output movement.
- Negative-stock prevention remains intact.
- Reservation-shortfall enforcement remains intact.
- Lot/stock integrity remains server-controlled.
- Serial-tracked BOM execution remains blocked by the existing `BOM_SERIAL_MAPPING_REQUIRED` safeguard.
- Product/archive, storage-location, permission, tenant, and user controls remain intact.
- The frontend readiness calculation is an early operator aid; it does not replace or bypass backend validation.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ADVANCED INVENTORY -> BOM EXECUTION — GENERIC SHARED SUCCESS TOAST
Canonical audit evidence:
- Successful BOM assembly produced the correct inline page message `Assembly completed and stock movements recorded.`
- The global/shared toast simultaneously said generic `Item created successfully.`
- Audit explicitly recorded this as misleading and requested assembly-specific feedback.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-026:
- `tenantMutationSuccessMessage()` had no route-specific handling for:
  - POST `/inventory-capabilities/boms`;
  - POST `/inventory-capabilities/boms/:id/execute`.
- The shared mutation-feedback layer therefore fell through to the generic POST `Item created successfully.` message.

Surgical correction — operation-specific shared feedback:
- POST `/inventory-capabilities/boms` now reports `BOM created successfully.`.
- POST `/inventory-capabilities/boms/:id/execute` reads the submitted direction and reports:
  - Assembly -> `Assembly completed and stock movements recorded.`;
  - Disassembly -> `Disassembly completed and stock movements recorded.`.
- Added complete five-language tenant catalog rows for the new full-message feedback and new BOM readiness guidance.

Important non-change:
- BOM create/execute API routes are unchanged.
- No stock quantity, movement, BOM definition, audit, reservation, lot, serial, or permission behavior changed.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 029 — ADVANCED INVENTORY LOCATION-HIERARCHY PARENT ELIGIBILITY CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-028 + Backend surgical-fix-027
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Rechecked the earlier Units of measure persistence failure before changing source. Current cumulative source now has an internally consistent UoM write/read contract: POST writes `product_uom_conversions`, GET reads the same tenant/product table, write transactions commit before success response, and the frontend invalidates the exact UoM query. No current source-backed discard/readback defect was identified, so no blind persistence patch was fabricated in this batch.
- Took the next confirmed audit finding that remains directly present in current source: Location hierarchy Parent dropdown still offered locations that the backend will always reject as parents.
- Frontend-only surgical correction; Backend surgical-fix-027 hierarchy validation remains unchanged and authoritative.

ADVANCED INVENTORY -> LOCATION HIERARCHY — PARENT DROPDOWN OFFERED STRUCTURALLY INVALID CHOICES
Canonical audit evidence:
- The production Parent selector offered legacy Storage/Bin locations as possible parents.
- Backend correctly rejected an ineligible legacy Storage parent with `LOCATION_PARENT_MUST_BE_CONTAINER`.
- Audit explicitly recorded this as a separate UX defect after the hierarchy-save serialization root cause: frontend should filter or disable ineligible parent choices while backend validation remains authoritative.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-028:
- HierarchyPanel rendered Parent options with only one filter: `location.id !== selectedLocationId`.
- It did not account for:
  - Warehouse must be top level;
  - Bin and legacy Storage are leaf/non-container parent types;
  - broader-to-more-specific hierarchy ordering (Warehouse -> Zone -> Aisle -> Rack -> Shelf -> Bin).
- Therefore the UI invited selections that Backend 027 necessarily rejects later.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/InventoryCapabilitiesPage.tsx
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts
- scripts/check-location-hierarchy-parent-eligibility-surgical-fix-v349272.mjs (new)
- package.json

Surgical correction:
- Added one explicit frontend parent-eligibility rule aligned with the existing backend hierarchy contract.
- Warehouses now expose Top level only.
- Bin and legacy Storage locations are no longer offered as valid parents.
- For Zone/Aisle/Rack/Shelf/Bin, only structurally broader parent levels are offered.
- Legacy Storage children may still be placed beneath valid container types, matching the current backend contract.
- The selected location itself is never offered as its own parent.
- Eligible Parent options now include the candidate's hierarchy type so users can understand why it is available.
- Added concise Parent-field guidance explaining the filtering.

Existing-invalid-parent handling:
- If historical/current data already points at a parent that is not eligible for the selected location type, the frontend does NOT silently rewrite the relationship.
- The existing parent remains visible as a disabled `Current parent is not eligible for this type` option.
- Save hierarchy is disabled while that known-invalid parent remains selected.
- The UI tells the user to choose an eligible parent or Top level before saving.

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- Backend hierarchy validation was NOT removed or relaxed.
- Backend continues to enforce:
  - Warehouse top-level rule;
  - Bin/legacy-Storage cannot contain children;
  - broad-to-specific level ordering;
  - cycle prevention;
  - leaf-has-children protection;
  - optimistic version control;
  - tenant and permission scope.
- No location hierarchy is changed automatically by the new frontend filtering.
- No stock is moved or mutated by this correction.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

REGRESSION PROTECTION
Frontend added:
- scripts/check-location-hierarchy-parent-eligibility-surgical-fix-v349272.mjs
- package command `check:inventory-location-hierarchy-parent-eligibility-surgical-fix-v349272`

New Batch 029 guard verifies:
- frontend hierarchy rank matches backend ordering;

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 031 — ADVANCED INVENTORY API-KEY ONE-TIME SECRET HYGIENE CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-030 + Backend surgical-fix-027
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Rechecked earlier Advanced Inventory UoM and Custom Fields runtime failures before editing. Current cumulative save/read paths no longer expose the historical source-level serialization defects, so no unsupported persistence change was fabricated.
- Rechecked the historical variant-parent defect; current frontend filters existing variants and Backend 027 rejects variant-on-variant parents, so it was not re-fixed.
- Took the next confirmed audit finding that remains directly present in Frontend 030: after rotating an API key and then revoking that same key, the one-time secret banner remained visible.
- Frontend-only surgical correction; API-key generation, rotation, revocation, authentication and audit behavior remain backend-owned and unchanged.

ADVANCED INVENTORY -> APIs & INTEGRATIONS — REVOKED API KEY LEFT ITS ONE-TIME SECRET VISIBLE
Canonical audit evidence:
- Controlled API-key rotation succeeded and exposed the replacement secret once.
- The same API key was then revoked successfully and its row changed to Revoked.
- Management actions disappeared as expected, but the previously revealed rotated secret banner remained visible.
- Audit classified this as a security/UX hygiene defect because the page continued displaying a credential belonging to a now-revoked client, creating confusion and unnecessary secret exposure.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-030:
- IntegrationsPanel stored the disclosed secret as a plain `string | null` in `revealedKey`.
- Create/rotate replaced that string, but the state did not remember which API client owned the secret.
- `revokeClient.onSuccess` refreshed the API-client list but never cleared the disclosed secret.
- Therefore the banner could outlive the credential lifecycle that produced it.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/InventoryCapabilitiesPage.tsx
- scripts/check-api-key-secret-hygiene-surgical-fix-v349274.mjs (new)
- package.json

Surgical correction:
- One-time API-key secret state now stores both:
  - the secret value;
  - the owning API-client id.
- API-key creation records the newly created client id with the one-time secret.
- API-key rotation records the rotated client id with the new one-time secret.
- Successful revoke receives the exact client that was revoked.
- If the currently displayed one-time secret belongs to that revoked client, the secret state is cleared immediately.
- The existing one-time disclosure warning remains intact.
- The table continues to hide management actions for non-active/revoked clients.

Important non-change / security boundary:
- No raw API secret is persisted to normal frontend records.
- Backend key generation was not changed.
- Backend rotation was not changed.
- Backend revocation still sets status=revoked, revoked_at, revoked_by_user_id and revoke_reason.
- Optimistic version control on revoke remains intact.
- Tenant API authentication still accepts only clients where status='active' and revoked_at IS NULL.
- Existing action-specific shared feedback remains intact:
  - API key secret rotated successfully.
  - API key revoked successfully.
- No API permission/scope, allowlist, expiry, tenant or role behavior changed.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 033 — ADVANCED INVENTORY CONNECTION REGISTRY LIFECYCLE CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-032 + Backend surgical-fix-027
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Took the confirmed Advanced Inventory -> APIs & integrations -> Connection registry lifecycle/cleanup gap.
- Audit evidence showed connection-registry records could be created and edited but exposed only Edit; obsolete/test records could not be disabled or removed from the UI.
- Inspected the current data model before editing. The existing connection table already supports status values `configured`, `disabled`, and `error`, and the Advanced Inventory overview already excludes disabled connections from the active external-connection count.
- Chose reversible Disable / Enable lifecycle handling rather than destructive deletion so historical registry records remain visible.

ADVANCED INVENTORY -> CONNECTION REGISTRY — NO LIFECYCLE ACTION
Canonical audit evidence:
- Controlled test record `Audit Test Connection` persisted successfully and could be edited.
- The registry row exposed only Edit.
- Audit classified this as a lifecycle/cleanup gap because an obsolete or test registry record could not be removed from active use from the page.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative source:
- Frontend rendered only Edit for every connection row.
- Shared POST `/inventory-capabilities/connections` validation accepted a status field, but defaulted it to `configured` for every request.
- Backend create path understood `configured`, `disabled`, and `error`.
- Backend UPDATE path did not update status at all.
- Therefore the existing status model could not be used as a controlled lifecycle action from the tenant UI.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/InventoryCapabilitiesPage.tsx
- src/lib/api.ts
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts
- scripts/check-connection-registry-lifecycle-surgical-fix-v349275.mjs (new)
- package.json

Backend files changed:
- src/services/inventory/inventoryCapabilitiesService.js
- src/validations/inventoryCapabilities.validation.js
- tests/connection-registry-lifecycle-surgical-fix.test.js (new)

Surgical correction — lifecycle controls:
- Connection registry rows now expose direct Edit plus Disable/Enable actions for users with the existing write/govern permission.
- `configured` / `error` rows can be disabled.
- `disabled` rows can be enabled back to `configured`.
- No destructive delete endpoint was introduced.
- Lifecycle changes continue through the existing governed POST `/inventory-capabilities/connections` route and existing write locks/permission boundary.
- After a lifecycle change, the connection list and Advanced Inventory overview are refreshed.
- If the changed row was open in Edit mode, the edit form is cleared so stale row/version state is not left on screen.

Surgical correction — safe backend status semantics:
- Connection-save validation no longer silently defaults `status= configured` on every update request.
- Create behavior remains unchanged: when no explicit status is supplied, new records still default to `configured` inside the service.
- Update behavior now distinguishes:
  - status omitted -> preserve current stored status;
  - explicit `disabled` -> disable;
  - explicit `configured` -> enable;
  - explicit supported `error` remains supported by the data/service contract.
- SQL uses an explicit boolean guard so an ordinary metadata Edit does not accidentally reactivate a disabled connection.

Action-specific feedback:
- New connection -> `Connection saved successfully.`
- Metadata edit -> `Connection updated successfully.`
- Disable -> `Connection disabled successfully.`
- Enable -> `Connection enabled successfully.`
- All new messages and status-update error feedback were added to the five-language tenant catalog.

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- No external synchronization was started or added.
- No external credentials are resolved or executed by this correction.
- No connection record is physically deleted.
- Existing tenant permission `ENTERPRISE_INTEGRATIONS_GOVERN`, write locks, transaction guard and tenant scoping remain authoritative.
- API clients and webhooks are unchanged.
- Existing connection metadata fields and optimistic `expected_version` concurrency remain unchanged.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 035 — OUTBOUND ORDER DETAIL FOCUS / CROSS-ORDER PREVIEW ISOLATION CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-034 + Backend surgical-fix-033
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Took the next confirmed unresolved Page 26 Outbound findings after the Scanner/Shipments focus closure.
- Two defects share the same order-selection boundary and were corrected together:
  1. Open Details could load the correct Order details below the current viewport without bringing the operator to the opened record.
  2. Switching from one selected order to another cleared the document preview but did not clear the open email preview, allowing a Delivery Note/Order Confirmation email preview from Order A to remain visible while Order B was selected.
- Frontend-only surgical correction; backend outbound/order/document/email/stock behavior remains unchanged.

OUTBOUND -> ORDERS -> OPEN DETAILS — OPENED RECORD COULD REMAIN OUTSIDE VIEWPORT
Canonical audit evidence:
- Open Details for dispatched order OUT-20260919-4C2F7095 loaded the correct detail panel and activity/document context.
- The operator had to manually scroll down to discover the newly opened Order details section.
- Audit classified the order opening as functional PASS with viewport/navigation UX FAIL.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-034:
- Order card Open Details only toggled `selectedOrderId`, reset the audit page, and cleared `documentPreview`.
- The rendered Order details section had no focus ref, no programmatic focusability, and no scroll/focus effect tied to selected-order changes.

Surgical correction:
- Added one dedicated `orderDetailRef` targeting the existing Order details workspace.
- The Order details section is now programmatically focusable with `tabIndex=-1` without adding a normal keyboard tab stop.
- Added an accessible `Order details` label to the focus target.
- After `selectedOrderId` changes to a real order, a post-render effect:
  - smoothly scrolls the Order details panel into view;
  - moves programmatic focus to the opened record without causing a second scroll jump.
- Added `scroll-margin-top: 104px` so the focused workspace is not positioned beneath the fixed tenant header.
- Added visible focus treatment for the focused details workspace.

Important non-change:
- Order filtering, pagination, draft editing, confirmation/reservation, picking, packing, dispatch, cancellation, documents, communications, attachments and stock behavior are unchanged.
- No order mutation occurs merely from opening/focusing details.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

OUTBOUND -> ORDERS — EMAIL PREVIEW FROM PREVIOUS ORDER COULD LEAK INTO NEW ORDER CONTEXT
Canonical audit evidence:
- Delivery Note email preview for OUT-20260919-4C2F7095 was left open.
- Operator then opened dispatched order OUT-20260903-5E8B2F04.
- The selected order changed correctly, but the old email preview remained visible inside the newly selected order detail.
- Audit classified this as a CRITICAL/MAJOR wrong-order stale-state/context-isolation defect and explicitly warned not to test Send Email from the stale state.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-034:
- Order card Open Details cleared `documentPreview` but did not clear `emailCompose`.
- The shared `emailCompose` renderer is displayed inside whichever order detail is currently selected when the compose document is not a return receipt.
- Therefore a compose state created under one order could survive a selected-order change and render under another order.
- Pending attachment-file state was also shared across selected order context and could remain selected when switching records.

Surgical correction:
- Added one centralized `clearOrderTransientState()` helper that clears:
  - order document preview;
  - order email compose/preview state;
  - pending attachment file selection.
- Added one `toggleOrderDetails(orderId)` transition helper.
- Every order-card Open Details / Close Details transition now clears the previous order-scoped transient state before changing the selected order.
- The detail-panel Close Details control uses the same cleanup path.
- This prevents a document/email/upload action prepared under Order A from remaining visible/prepared when Order B becomes the selected record.

Important safety boundary:
- Actual email preview creation remains read-only and still suppresses generic mutation feedback.
- Actual Send Email remains a separate explicit action and was not changed.
- No document or communication record is deleted by the UI cleanup; only local transient compose/preview state is cleared.
- Email history, stored PDF documents and attachments already persisted on the server remain intact.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
