SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 036 — USAGE LEDGER REVERSAL TERMINAL-STATE CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-035 + Backend surgical-fix-033
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Took the next confirmed unresolved Usage Ledger finding after the Outbound detail/context closure.
- Audit evidence showed an inventory-usage reversal completed successfully and persisted as Reversed, while the row action could remain stuck on `Reversing...`, including after subsequent page refresh/reconciliation.
- Frontend-only surgical correction; backend reversal/stock/audit semantics remain unchanged.

USAGE LEDGER -> REVERSE — COMPLETED ROW COULD REMAIN STUCK ON `REVERSING...`
Canonical audit evidence:
- Reverse successfully restored stock and the persisted ledger row showed Reversed with reversal timestamp/reason.
- Despite that terminal state, the action area continued to display `Reversing...`.
- Audit classified this as a confirmed frontend/UI state defect because transient mutation state contradicted the persisted completed state.

Current-source review before correction:
- The current cumulative source already prompts `Why are you reversing this usage entry?` and refuses a blank reason before calling the stock-changing reversal API.
- Therefore the earlier audit concern about immediate reversal without a reason/confirmation step is already absent in the current source and was not reimplemented.
- Root cause of the stale label remained present: `reversingUsageId` was derived from `reverseUsageMutation.variables?.usageLogId` without checking whether the mutation was still pending.
- TanStack Query retains the last mutation variables after completion, so the completed row ID could continue to match the transient rendering condition indefinitely.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/InventoryUsagePage.tsx
- src/pages/inventoryUsage/InventoryUsageDashboard.tsx
- scripts/check-inventory-usage-reversal-state-surgical-fix-v349278.mjs (new)
- package.json

Surgical correction:
- Reversal success callback is now async and waits for the Usage Ledger summary/list/exception/anomaly/impact/detail invalidations through `Promise.all` before the mutation leaves its pending reconciliation boundary.
- `reversingUsageId` is now supplied only while `reverseUsageMutation.isPending` is true.
- A persisted reversed row no longer renders the Reverse/Reversing action at all; its terminal Reversed status/reason remains visible in the status column.
- An active row still disables the Reverse button only while that exact row has an in-flight reversal.

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- Existing required reversal-reason prompt remains intact.
- Backend `INVENTORY_USAGE_REVERSE` permission enforcement remains intact.
- Backend already-reversed/idempotency protection remains intact.
- Stock restoration, compensating movement creation, audit linkage, usage history and reversal reason persistence were not changed.
- No backend file changed.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 038 — REQUISITIONS LINKED-RESERVATION STATE SYNCHRONIZATION CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-037 + Backend surgical-fix-033
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Re-reviewed the historical Requisitions -> Create linked reservation failure before changing code.
- Important source finding: Backend 033 intentionally supports requisition-linked reservations without a preselected source location by creating the reservation with allocation strategy `any_location`. Therefore the old audit suggestion to block creation until a source location is selected was NOT applied because it would remove a valid current workflow.
- Took the still-present current-source defect instead: successful linked-reservation creation had no frontend success reconciliation, no persistent linked-reservation lookup, no direct reservation handoff, and the Create linked reservation action could remain available after the backend had already created the reservation.
- Frontend-only surgical correction; reservation creation/allocation/stock semantics remain unchanged.

REQUISITIONS -> CREATE LINKED RESERVATION — SUCCESS STATE WAS NOT RECONCILED
Canonical audit evidence:
- During production testing, Create linked reservation could leave the operator without a reliable post-action state and required leaving Requisitions to confirm the created reservation in Reservations.
- Later verification proved the backend had created RSV-000004 even though the Requisitions workflow did not give a durable linked-reservation context.
- Audit explicitly identified this as an async action feedback/state synchronization problem and requested immediate success/failure reconciliation plus a visible linked-reservation identifier and handoff.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-037:
- `createLinkedReservationMutation` only defined `mutationFn`; it had no `onSuccess` reconciliation.
- Successful creation did not invalidate Requisitions detail/readiness/activity or Reservations list/summary/source/conflict queries.
- The only linked-reservation success context came from transient `createLinkedReservationMutation.data` rendered near the top of the page.
- Requisition selection itself did not query the Reservations API for an already-open requisition-owned reservation.
- `canCreateLinkedReservation` did not consider an existing linked reservation, so the action could still be presented after a successful backend create until another backend duplicate guard rejected a retry.
- No direct handoff to the exact created reservation was available from Requisitions.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/InventoryRequisitionsPage.tsx
- src/lib/api.ts
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts
- scripts/check-requisition-linked-reservation-sync-surgical-fix-v349280.mjs (new)
- package.json

Surgical correction — persistent linkage awareness:
- Added a selected-requisition linked-reservation query using the existing Reservations list endpoint filtered by:
  - source_type = requisition;
  - source_id = selected requisition ID.
- Closed reservation states (`fulfilled`, `released`, `expired`, `cancelled`) are ignored when deciding whether the requisition currently has an open linked reservation.
- The query is permission-gated by the existing Reservations read capability.
- A newly returned mutation result is scoped to the exact requisition that created it so transient mutation state cannot leak into another selected requisition.

Surgical correction — post-create reconciliation:
- After successful linked-reservation creation, the page now refreshes:
  - Requisitions queue/detail/summary/options/readiness/activity/stock-related queries through the existing `invalidateRequisitions()` path;
  - selected requisition linked-reservation lookup;
  - Reservations queue;
  - Reservations summary;
  - Reservations source summary;
  - reservation conflict state.
- This removes the old need to leave the page and manually search Reservations just to determine whether the create action succeeded.

Surgical correction — duplicate-action prevention / exact handoff:
- Once an open linked reservation is known, `Create linked reservation` is no longer offered for that requisition.
- For users with Reservations read permission, the page waits for the selected requisition linkage check before exposing a create action, preventing an avoidable duplicate-create race during initial readback.
- The Selected request panel persistently shows:
  - linked reservation number;
  - current reservation status.
- Added `Open linked reservation`, which deep-links directly to `/inventory-reservations?reservationId=<exact id>` using the Reservations page's existing exact-record query contract.

Action-specific feedback:
- POST `/inventory-reservations/from-requisition/:id` now reports `Linked reservation created successfully.` instead of the generic POST success message.
- Added five-language catalog rows for:
  - `Linked reservation created successfully.`
  - `Open linked reservation`

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- Backend surgical-fix-033 was NOT changed.
- Requisition-linked reservation creation still permits source-less `any_location` reservations when the requisition has no source storage location.
- Existing backend duplicate-open-reservation safeguard `REQUISITION_RESERVATION_ALREADY_EXISTS` remains authoritative.
- Existing owner/cross-request permission boundary remains authoritative.
- Existing eligible requisition statuses remain unchanged.
- Reservation activation/allocation semantics remain unchanged.
- No stock is allocated or moved merely by the new frontend reconciliation/deep-link behavior.
- Requisition fulfillment and linked-reservation integrity checks remain unchanged.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

REGRESSION PROTECTION

- persistent linkage query is keyed to the selected requisition;

- closed reservations are excluded from active linkage detection;

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 039 — RESERVATION ALLOCATION VERSION / SERVER-ERROR CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-038 + Backend surgical-fix-033
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Took the confirmed Reservations allocation blocking defect recorded during the requisition-linked reservation flow.
- Production evidence showed Allocate on an active requisition-linked reservation returned an unexpected server error even though projected free stock was sufficient and no allocation conflict existed.
- Root cause was confirmed directly in Backend surgical-fix-033 and corrected surgically.

RESERVATIONS -> ALLOCATE — UNDEFINED expectedVersion CAUSED SERVER ERROR
Canonical audit evidence:
- RSV-000004 was Active, requisition-linked, requested Coca Cola 0.33L quantity 1, reserved 0.
- Projected free stock showed sufficient inventory at Main Warehouse and Kitchen Store.
- Allocate returned `Unexpected server error` and no allocation persisted.
- Refresh confirmed reservation remained unchanged and no allocation audit event was created.

Root cause confirmed in current backend source:
- `allocateReservation()` referenced `expectedVersion` before the normal reservation lifecycle checks.
- `expectedVersion` was not declared in the function signature or body.
- Therefore an allocation request could throw a JavaScript ReferenceError before stock availability/allocation logic ran.
- This exactly explains a generic 500/server-error response despite sufficient stock.

Backend correction:
- `allocateReservation()` now reads optional `expected_version` from the validated request body.
- Existing optimistic version-conflict logic is preserved and now functions normally.
- Allocation validation accepts optional positive integer `expected_version`.
- If no version is supplied, allocation remains valid and follows the existing locked transactional allocation path.
- If a stale version is supplied, the service returns the intended 409 `VERSION_CONFLICT` instead of a server error.

Frontend correction:
- Reservations UI sends the currently displayed reservation version when the user invokes Allocate.
- The version is scoped to the exact selected reservation ID.
- Existing `allow_partial = true` UI allocation behavior remains unchanged.

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- Any-location reservation allocation remains supported.
- Specific-location pinning remains unchanged.
- Projected free stock selection logic remains unchanged.
- Partial-allocation behavior remains unchanged.
- Reservation status transitions and audit logging remain unchanged.
- Requisition linked-reservation creation behavior remains unchanged.
- No stock is moved merely by the version-handling correction.

REGRESSION PROTECTION
Backend added:
- tests/inventory-reservation-allocation-version-surgical-fix.test.js
  - allocation without expected_version no longer crashes on an undefined variable;
  - stale expected_version returns 409 VERSION_CONFLICT;
  - allocation validation accepts valid expected_version and rejects invalid values.

Frontend added:
- scripts/check-reservation-allocation-version-surgical-fix-v349281.mjs

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 041 — EXECUTION REQUEST SINGLE-SUCCESS FEEDBACK CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-040 + Backend surgical-fix-039
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Moved from Reservations into Execution Requests after Batch 040.
- Took the confirmed repeated-notification defect recorded during Execution Request submit, approve, controlled execute, and complete-without-change actions.
- Current source review showed the same structural duplication also affected the page-owned reject, retry, cancel, and request-creation flows.
- Frontend-only surgical correction; Execution Request lifecycle, permission, review, execution, retry, audit, and product-mutation behavior remain unchanged.

EXECUTION REQUESTS — ONE ACTION PRODUCED TWO DIFFERENT SUCCESS NOTIFICATIONS
Canonical audit evidence:
- Submit for review produced both `Execution Request submitted successfully.` and `Execution request submitted for review.`.
- Approval produced both `Execution Request approved successfully.` and `Execution request approved.`.
- Controlled execution produced both `Execution Request executed successfully.` and `Controlled execution completed.`.
- Complete without change produced both `Execution Request no-op completed successfully.` and `Request completed without a business-data change.`.
- Audit repeatedly classified this as noisy/redundant success feedback: one user action should produce one clear confirmation.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-040:
- Shared apiRequest mutation feedback still correctly maps Execution Request POST lifecycle routes to action-specific generic success messages.
- ExecutionRequestsPage also publishes its own page-aware success message immediately after the same mutation completes.
- Because the two strings are intentionally different, the existing near-simultaneous identical-toast dedupe guard cannot collapse them.
- The duplication was therefore deterministic for page-owned lifecycle actions rather than a random rendering issue.

Frontend files changed:
- src/pages/ExecutionRequestsPage.tsx
- scripts/check-execution-request-feedback-dedupe-surgical-fix-v349283.mjs (new)
- package.json

Surgical correction:
- Kept the existing page-owned business-facing success/error messages as the single final feedback source inside ExecutionRequestsPage.
- Added `skipMutationFeedback: true` to page-owned Execution Request mutations so the shared apiRequest layer does not publish a second success toast for the same action.
- Applied consistently to:
  - recommendation-generated request creation;
  - controlled product request creation;
  - submit for review;
  - approve;
  - reject;
  - controlled execute;
  - complete without business-data change;
  - prepare controlled retry;
  - cancel request.
- Shared API feedback mappings were deliberately left intact for other callers of the same routes.
- Error handling remains page-owned and unchanged.

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- No backend source changed.
- No request payload, route, permission, workflow status, review state, execution adapter, separation-of-duties rule, retry boundary, cancellation rule, audit event, before/after evidence, or product mutation changed.
- Global toast dedupe behavior remains unchanged.
- Existing shared API action-specific feedback remains available to non-ExecutionRequestsPage callers.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 043 — EXECUTION REQUESTS VISIBLE REQUEST-TYPE SEARCH CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-042 + Backend surgical-fix-039
Resulting baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-042 + Backend surgical-fix-043
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued the canonical tenant-audit correction sequence from the current cumulative source.
- Reconciled the remaining Execution Requests search finding before moving on to Execution Tasks.
- Audit evidence showed broad search worked for Coca Cola, Operations Manager, Product Min Stock Update and System Recommendation, but the exact visible request type `Standard Cost Update` unexpectedly returned zero results.
- Current source inspection confirmed the defect still existed in Backend surgical-fix-039.
- Backend-only search correction; frontend workflow/UI behavior remains unchanged.

EXECUTION REQUESTS -> SEARCH BY VISIBLE REQUEST-TYPE LABEL
Canonical audit symptom:
- Search `Standard Cost Update` returned 0 even though requests of that visible type were present.
- Other tested free-text searches worked, indicating a request-type normalization mismatch rather than a general search failure.

Root cause confirmed in Backend surgical-fix-039:
- The frontend human-readable label for stored request type `cost_standard_update` is `Standard cost update`.
- Backend free-text search matched the raw request type and `REPLACE(er.request_type, '_', ' ')`.
- `cost_standard_update` therefore normalized to `cost standard update`, while the visible UI label is `standard cost update`.
- Because the word order differs, searching the exact visible label could not match the stored type through the existing normalization path.

Surgical correction:
- Extended Execution Request backend search with a hardcoded CASE mapping from every supported request_type to the same human-readable English label used by the Execution Requests page.
- `cost_standard_update` now searches as `Standard cost update`, so the visible label matches correctly.
- The mapping also keeps the other supported request types aligned with their human-readable labels:
  - Cost review;
  - Minimum stock update;
  - Product pricing update;
  - Supplier review;
  - Inventory review;
  - System recommendation.
- Existing search behavior remains intact for:
  - request UUID;
  - raw request_type;
  - underscore-to-space request type;
  - status/execution status;
  - safe payload text;
  - gate snapshot;
  - involved users;
  - linked product name.

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- No frontend source changed.
- No request creation, submission, review, approval, rejection, cancellation, execution, no-op completion, retry, permission, audit, separation-of-duties or product mutation behavior changed.
- No database schema or migration changed.
- No result ordering, pagination, tenant scoping or existing filters changed.
- Search remains tenant-scoped and parameterized.

EXECUTION TASKS -> ASSIGNMENT/LIFECYCLE AUDIT TRAIL STAYS STALE

Root cause confirmed in cumulative source:
Backend surgical-fix-043:
- `transitionExecutionTask()` already calls `logAuditEvent()` for every lifecycle transition.
- Assignment is recorded as `execution_task.assign` with task code/type, previous status, resulting status, assignee and source context.
- `getExecutionTaskAuditTrail()` already reads tenant-scoped `audit_logs` rows for the selected execution task.

Frontend surgical-fix-042:
- Execution Tasks loads `/execution-tasks/${selected.id}/audit?limit=100` inside an effect whose dependency list was only `[canRead, selected?.id]`.
- Assignment updates the selected task object but keeps the same task ID.
- Therefore the lifecycle/status/timestamp UI updated, but the audit-loading effect did not run again for the same task ID.
- This exactly explains the production symptom without requiring any backend audit rewrite.

Surgical correction:
- The task-audit effect now also watches `selected?.updated_at`.
- Every successful same-task lifecycle mutation updates the task `updated_at` timestamp, so the selected task audit trail is fetched again after assignment and other lifecycle transitions.
- Selecting a different task still reloads audit by task ID as before.
- The existing task-specific audit endpoint, audit renderer and tenant permission boundaries are unchanged.

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- No backend files changed.
- No task transition SQL changed.
- No assignment permission or assignee-capability rules changed.
- No Start/Block/Unblock/Complete/Cancel behavior changed.
- No audit-log schema or audit event payload changed.
- No stock-changing workflow behavior changed.
- Backend surgical-fix-043 remains the backend baseline.

EXECUTION TASKS -> GENERIC `ITEM CREATED SUCCESSFULLY.` AFTER TASK ACTIONS

- The shared/global toast nevertheless reported `Item created successfully.` even though no generic item was created.
- Audit explicitly requested task-specific confirmation such as `Task assigned successfully.`.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-044:
- `ExecutionTasksPage.tsx` already provides a useful inline business message after task lifecycle actions: `{taskCode} is now {status}.`.

Date: 2026-10-07

- Current source does not contain the historical closed-task exclusion root cause, so no speculative Execution Tasks patch was manufactured.
- Moved to Automation Schedules and fixed the first concrete source-backed defect still present: schedule-specific result/audit state leaking across record creation.

EXECUTION TASKS -> COMPLETED TASK HISTORY FINDING RECONCILIATION
Historical audit symptom:
- Completed task TASK-MUMWHRVE-W3EVZD disappeared from normal Execution Tasks retrieval even with `Open tasks only` unchecked and Status = Completed / All statuses.

Current-source inspection:
- Frontend surgical-fix-045 explicitly sends `open_only=false` when the checkbox is off.
- Backend surgical-fix-043 validation accepts the boolean false value.
- `listExecutionTasks()` only adds `status = ANY(OPEN_EXECUTION_TASK_STATUSES)` when `filters.open_only` is truthy.
- Completion changes the same `execution_tasks` row to `status = completed` and records `completed_at`; current source does not delete/archive that row from the normal registry.

Decision:
- NO CODE CHANGE for this historical symptom.
- A source-backed exclusion defect is not present in the cumulative code.
- The old production symptom requires deployment/database retesting before any further change.
- This follows the project rule: do not fake a patch when the historical root cause cannot be reproduced in current source.

AUTOMATION SCHEDULES -> STALE DETAIL / AUDIT STATE ACROSS SCHEDULE CREATION
Canonical audit evidence:
- After loading automation-test-001 audit history, the user created automation-test-002.
- The newly selected automation-test-002 temporarily displayed 4 schedule audit events inherited from automation-test-001.
- After explicitly loading automation-test-002 audit history, the count correctly became 1.
- Audit conclusion: schedule-specific audit/detail state was not being cleared when selection changed through the create path.

Root cause confirmed in Frontend surgical-fix-045:
- `loadScheduleDetail()` correctly cleared `dryRunResult`, `manualRunResult`, and `auditPack` when switching to an existing schedule.
- `createSchedule()` selected the new schedule but did not clear any of those previous schedule-specific result states.
- Because `auditPack` rendering depends only on whether the state is non-null, evidence from the previously selected schedule could remain visible under the new schedule until another audit load replaced it.

Surgical correction:
- Immediately after selecting a newly created schedule, `createSchedule()` now clears:
  - `dryRunResult`;
  - `manualRunResult`;
  - `auditPack`.
- The new schedule therefore starts with a clean detail/result context.
- Existing explicit detail-selection behavior remains unchanged and continues clearing the same states.

Important non-change / safety boundary:
- No backend files changed.
- No Automation Schedule endpoint changed.
- No schedule create/update/pause/resume/disable behavior changed.
- No automation runner behavior changed.
- No Execution Request creation/execution behavior changed.
- No inventory-changing behavior changed.
- No permissions or tenant scoping changed.
- Backend surgical-fix-043 remains authoritative and unchanged.

AUTOMATION SCHEDULES -> GENERIC/STALE `AUTOMATION SCHEDULE CREATED SUCCESSFULLY.` AFTER LIFECYCLE ACTIONS
Canonical audit evidence:
- Creating automation-test-002 correctly produced `Automation schedule created successfully.`.
- Activating that existing schedule also incorrectly produced the same create-style toast.
- Pausing the schedule again incorrectly produced `Automation schedule created successfully.`.
- Disabling the schedule again incorrectly produced `Automation schedule created successfully.`.
- Audit explicitly required action-specific success wording after lifecycle transitions.

Root cause confirmed in cumulative Frontend surgical-fix-046:
- `AutomationSchedulesPage.tsx` already owns useful detailed inline success messages for lifecycle and runner actions.
- Shared mutation feedback in `src/lib/api.ts` recognized `/automation-schedules` only at the generic label level.
- POST lifecycle/action routes had no dedicated shared-success mappings.
- Therefore `/pause`, `/resume`, `/disable`, `/dry-run`, `/run`, runner run-once and safety-review acknowledgement all fell through to the generic POST branch, which converts the `Automation schedule` label into `Automation schedule created successfully.`.

Surgical correction:

  - Create -> `Automation schedule created successfully.`
- Added action-specific lifecycle feedback:
  - Pause -> `Automation schedule paused successfully.`
  - Resume/Activate -> `Automation schedule activated successfully.`
  - Disable -> `Automation schedule disabled successfully.`
- Protected other Automation Schedule POST actions from the same generic-create fallback:
  - Dry run / Preview -> `Automation schedule preview completed successfully.`
  - Manual schedule run -> `Automation schedule run completed successfully.`
  - Runner run-once -> `Due schedule processing completed successfully.`
  - Unsafe-output review acknowledgement -> `Automation safety review acknowledged successfully.`
- Added complete five-language tenant translations for all of these shared feedback strings.

SURGICAL CORRECTION BATCH 048 — AUTOMATION SCHEDULE CREATE-REQUEST SAFETY PRE-GATING
Date: 2026-10-07
Baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-047 + Backend surgical-fix-043
Resulting baseline: Frontend surgical-fix-048 + Backend surgical-fix-043
================================================================================

Scope
- Continued in canonical audit order within Automation Schedules.
- Reconciled the confirmed production-safety UX defect where schedule-level `Create request` remained clickable while request preparation was locked off.
- Backend safety behavior was already correct and remained authoritative; this batch prevents the tenant UI from intentionally sending a guaranteed-fail request into that backend lock.
- Frontend-only correction; no schedule lifecycle, execution-request approval/execution, inventory, stock, or automatic-runner configuration changed.

AUTOMATION SCHEDULES -> CREATE REQUEST REMAINS CLICKABLE WHILE REQUEST PREPARATION IS OFF
Canonical audit evidence:
- Active schedule `automation-test-002` showed `Create request` as available.
- Create request -> confirmation -> Confirm returned: `Automation schedule request creation is locked by the production safety configuration.`
- Automation safety then confirmed:
  * Request preparation = Off;
  * Background scheduler = Not running;
  * Automatic execution = Off.
- `Process due schedules now` was already correctly disabled under this safety posture.
- Audit required Create request to be pre-gated/disabled with a clear business-facing explanation instead of allowing a guaranteed-fail confirmation flow and surfacing a technical error / Request ID.

CURRENT-SOURCE ROOT CAUSE — FRONTEND 047
- Registry-row Create request checked only saving state, disabled schedule state, and permissions.
- Selected-detail Create request checked the same boundaries.
- Neither action checked the backend production safety lock before opening the manual-run confirmation.
- `/automation-schedules/runner-status` was loaded only after opening Automation safety details, so the normal schedule action area did not have the safety lock available for pre-gating.
- Backend 043 already exposes `production_safety_lock` with:
  * global_disable;
  * tenant_request_creation_enabled;
  * automatic runner flags;
  * request_creation_allowed_for_mode.
- Backend 043 also already rejects locked manual runs with `AUTOMATION_REQUEST_CREATION_LOCKED`.
- Therefore no backend safety rule needed changing.

CORRECTION
Frontend:
- Core Automation Schedules load now also retrieves `/automation-schedules/runner-status`.
- Added manual request-creation safety state derived from:
  * `production_safety_lock.global_disable === false`; and
  * `production_safety_lock.tenant_request_creation_enabled === true`.
- This intentionally does NOT reuse the stricter automatic-runner `request_creation_enabled` flag for manual Create request, because Backend 043 supports manual request creation separately from controlled automatic due processing.
- Registry-row Create request is disabled when manual request preparation is locked.
- Selected-detail Create request is disabled under the same lock.
- Locked buttons expose a plain-language explanation before interaction.
- Selected schedule detail shows the same visible warning when request creation is locked.
- Added `requestManualRunConfirmation()` preflight so the confirmation modal cannot be opened through normal page flow while locked.
- `confirmAction()` rechecks the lock before the mutation as an additional UI guard.
- If runner safety status cannot be loaded, Create request fails closed with refresh guidance.
- Automation safety `Request preparation` now reflects the manual request-preparation lock used by schedule-level Create request.
- `Process due schedules now` remains unchanged on `runnerStatus.request_creation_enabled`, preserving its stricter controlled automatic-runner gate.
- Added five-language translations for:
  * request preparation turned-off guidance;
  * request-creation safety-status unavailable guidance.
- Frontend `AutomationRunnerStatusResponse` now types the existing backend `production_safety_lock` payload.

Safety / non-change boundaries:
- No backend source changed.
- No tenant permission changed.
- No Automation Schedule transition changed.
- No request approval or execution rule changed.
- No automatic-runner enablement flag changed.
- No stock, product, shipment, supplier, or movement mutation behavior changed.
- Backend 043 remains final authority for safety enforcement.

BATCH 049 — AUTOMATION SCHEDULES DISABLE-REASON VALIDATION UX
Date: 2026-10-07
================================================================================

BASELINE
- Frontend: hotel-inventory-frontend_surgical-fix-048.zip
- Backend: hotel-inventory-backend_surgical-fix-043.zip
- Backend changed in this batch: NO

AUDIT DEFECT
Canonical audit retained this Automation Schedules defect:
- Disable opens a modal with a required Disable reason.
- Confirm remained enabled while the required reason was empty.
- No inline validation/help explained the requirement before confirmation.
- The audit recommended disabling Confirm until the trimmed reason is valid or providing immediate inline validation with focus.

CURRENT-SOURCE RECONCILIATION
The defect was still present in Frontend 048.
- Disable reason textarea declared minLength={3}.
- confirmAction() rejected confirmationText.trim().length < 3.
- But the modal Confirm button used disabled={saving} only.
Because the modal uses an explicit button handler rather than native form submission, textarea minLength did not prevent the action. The rule was therefore visible only after the user clicked Confirm.

ROOT CAUSE
The 3-character Disable-reason requirement existed only as post-click client validation. It was not represented in the action's enabled/disabled state or inline guidance.

FILES CHANGED
1. src/pages/AutomationSchedulesPage.tsx
2. package.json
3. scripts/check-automation-schedule-disable-validation-surgical-fix-v349290.mjs (new)

EXACT CORRECTION
- Disable reason textarea remains minLength=3 and maxLength=1000.
- Added required and aria-required="true".
- Added aria-describedby to connect the field to visible validation guidance.
- Added inline helper text using the existing translated key:
  "Enter a disable reason of at least 3 characters."
- Confirm is disabled when:
  confirmation.kind === 'disable' && confirmationText.trim().length < 3
- The disabled Confirm action also exposes the same requirement via title.
- Existing confirmAction() length validation remains intact as defense in depth.

SAFETY / NON-CHANGE BOUNDARIES
- No backend changes.
- No change to schedule disable endpoint or payload.
- No change to minimum valid reason length.
- No change to schedule lifecycle/state transitions.
- No change to permissions.
- No change to audit persistence.
- No change to automation runner/safety behavior.
- No change to request creation/execution behavior.
- No change to Batch 047 action-specific success feedback.

- the reason is exposed before interaction;
- non-Disable confirmation kinds are not blocked by this rule;

Date: 2026-10-07

BASELINE
- Frontend: hotel-inventory-frontend_surgical-fix-051.zip

AUDIT FINDINGS RECONCILED
The production audit repeatedly confirmed that Purchase Order lifecycle actions were functionally successful but shared mutation feedback described them as creation events:
- Submit for approval -> "Purchase order created successfully."
- Approve order -> "Purchase order created successfully."

The same audit also recorded the supplier-send preparation path showing two simultaneous misleading success messages while the Supplier Email Preview explicitly stated that nothing had been sent yet:
- "Purchase order created successfully."
- "Shipment created successfully."

ROOT CAUSE STILL PRESENT
- src/lib/api.ts had a Purchase Order mutation noun label but no exact route/action mapping, so POST /purchase-orders/:id/<action> fell through to the generic POST-created fallback.
- /purchase-orders/:id/create-shipment therefore incorrectly reported Purchase Order creation.
- /shipments/:id/supplier-email-preview is a POST but represents preview/preparation, so it incorrectly reported Shipment creation.
- Purchase Order draft creation and final supplier-email send already had authoritative page-owned success messages but did not suppress the shared mutation feedback layer.

  - Submit -> Purchase order submitted successfully.
  - Approve -> Purchase order approved successfully.
  - Cancel -> Purchase order cancelled successfully.
  - Close -> Purchase order closed successfully.
  - Reopen -> Purchase order reopened successfully.
- /purchase-orders/:id/create-shipment now reports the real stage: "Shipment prepared. Review the supplier email before sending."
- Purchase Order draft creation now suppresses shared mutation feedback because the page already owns the "PO draft created" success confirmation.
- Supplier email preview now suppresses shared mutation feedback because preview is not a Shipment creation result.

BATCH 055 — PURCHASE ORDERS REPEAT SUPPLIER-EMAIL / DUPLICATE-SEND PROTECTION
Date: 2026-10-08
================================================================================

BASELINE
- Frontend: hotel-inventory-frontend_surgical-fix-054.zip
- Backend: hotel-inventory-backend_surgical-fix-054.zip

AUDIT FINDING RECONCILED
After a Purchase Order supplier email had already been sent and persisted supplier-email evidence existed, the same Purchase Order detail still exposed Send to supplier for the current open receiving shipment. The audit classified this as a duplicate-send / duplicate-side-effect risk and explicitly instructed not to click it again before source inspection.

ROOT CAUSE STILL PRESENT
FRONTEND
- Frontend 054 based Send eligibility on approved PO state, remaining quantity, permissions, and delivery-date readiness only.
- It never consulted supplier-email evidence to determine whether the CURRENT OPEN receiving shipment had already been successfully emailed.

BACKEND
- Backend 054 required confirmed=true but did not reject another confirmed send after successful supplier-email evidence already existed for the same PO-linked shipment.
- A stale browser could therefore repeat the supplier communication.

- Send eligibility fails closed while the open shipment's supplier-email evidence is loading, refetching, or unavailable.
- If that current open shipment already has a sent supplier email, Send to supplier is replaced by a plain Supplier email already sent state directing the operator to the immutable evidence below.
- prepareSelectedPurchaseOrderEmail() repeats the sent-evidence and history-readiness checks before mutation as a defensive boundary.
- Old sent evidence from an earlier/completed shipment does not block a later new shipment for remaining PO quantity.
- New already-sent/safety wording added to all five tenant locales.

BACKEND

- No approval, receiving, stock, supplier, recommendation, quantity, or optimistic-version behavior changed.

BATCH 058 — PROCUREMENT RECOMMENDATIONS GENERATED-PO LOADED SPEND CURRENCY CONTINUITY
Date: 2026-10-08
================================================================================

BASELINE
- Frontend: hotel-inventory-frontend_surgical-fix-057.zip
- Backend: hotel-inventory-backend_surgical-fix-056.zip

AUDIT FINDING RECONCILED
The Procurement Recommendations Purchase order drafts area displayed three generated PO rows with visible spend of EUR 108.00, EUR 29.75, and EUR 57.60, but the Loaded spend card showed EUR 137.75. The visible EUR 57.60 row was omitted from the summary even though it appeared in the same loaded table.

ROOT CAUSE STILL PRESENT
- Generated-PO review selected purchase_orders.currency directly.
- Legacy/generated POs can have null stored currency while the tenant has a configured inventory currency.
- The frontend row renderer falls back to the active tenant currency, so the row still appears as a normal EUR amount.
- The summary currency breakdown does not treat a null-currency row as EUR, and the frontend breakdown renderer ignores null currency entries.
- Result: the row was visible but could silently disappear from Loaded spend.

FILES CHANGED — FRONTEND
- None. Frontend remains cumulative surgical-fix-057.

FILES CHANGED — BACKEND

2. tests/procurement-po-draft-loaded-spend-currency-surgical-fix.test.js (new)

EXACT CORRECTION
- Generated PO review now resolves currency with:
  COALESCE(po.currency, (SELECT inventory_currency FROM tenants WHERE id = po.tenant_id)) AS currency
- Explicit PO currencies remain authoritative.
- Legacy null-currency generated POs use the same tenant currency for both row presentation and Loaded spend aggregation.
- Mixed currencies remain separated; no cross-currency arithmetic is introduced.

SAFETY / NON-CHANGE BOUNDARIES
- No historical row was rewritten.
- No migration/backfill was added.
- No amounts, PO lifecycle, recommendation decisions, shipment behavior, stock behavior, supplier-email behavior, or permissions changed.
- Frontend source remains unchanged.

BATCH 059 — PROCUREMENT RECOMMENDATIONS ACTIVE DECISION SCOPE CLOSURE
Date: 2026-10-08
================================================================================

BASELINE
- Frontend: hotel-inventory-frontend_surgical-fix-057.zip
- Backend: hotel-inventory-backend_surgical-fix-058.zip

AUDIT FINDING RECONCILED
After one recommendation was deferred and another rejected, the Procurement Recommendations page still described all three visible rows as "active recommendations". The top cards simultaneously showed Active recommendations = 3 and Critical = 3 even though only the approved recommendation remained operationally active. Deferred and Rejected rows therefore inflated active queue/count/severity/spend semantics.

ROOT CAUSE STILL PRESENT
- Frontend 057 intentionally requests active_only=true for the normal Recommendation queue.
- Backend 058 defined isActiveReplenishmentRecommendation() only as:
  - recommended reorder quantity > 0; and
  - no converted purchase order.
- decision_status was not considered.
- Therefore rejected and deferred recommendations remained inside the active-only scope and flowed into pagination totals, Active recommendations, Critical/High counts, Recommendation spend, execution dashboard risk/supplier summaries, and other helpers that explicitly consume active procurement work.

FILES CHANGED — FRONTEND
- None. Frontend remains cumulative surgical-fix-057.

FILES CHANGED — BACKEND

EXACT CORRECTION
The canonical active recommendation predicate now requires all of the following:
- recommended_reorder_quantity > 0;
- no converted_purchase_order_id; and
- decision_status is not rejected or deferred.

Resulting semantics:
- Pending recommendation with positive reorder quantity: ACTIVE.
- Approved but not yet converted recommendation: ACTIVE.
- Rejected recommendation: NOT ACTIVE.
- Deferred recommendation: NOT ACTIVE.
- Converted recommendation: NOT ACTIVE.
- Zero-quantity recommendation: NOT ACTIVE.

Historical/review access is preserved because callers that need decision history can still request active_only=false. No decisions are deleted or rewritten.

BATCH 071 — INVENTORY REQUISITIONS VISIBLE IN CENTRAL APPROVALS SCREEN
Date: 2026-10-09
================================================================================

BASELINE
Frontend 070 + Backend 070, verified against the two prior cumulative source ZIPs. Backend remains 070; there is no backend 071.

AUDIT FINDING
2026-09-30 REQ-000003 was submitted as an inventory requisition for Housekeeping. Manager could Approve/Reject directly in Requisitions, but Inventory Controls -> Approvals said no items were waiting despite a Housekeeping -> Manager Department requisition approval rule. This was a real incomplete work-queue visibility defect.
The original test requisition was later directly approved and fulfilled, so it must NOT be expected to appear as an outstanding approval in a new live retest; use a new submitted requisition.

EXACT ROOT CAUSE
Two independent requisition workflows were conflated in the historical UI expectations:
1. Operational inventory_requisitions (the Requisitions page) stores status=submitted and approves with inventory_requisitions.approve; self-approval restrictions and threshold-approval notes are enforced there.
2. Department requisitions (Inventory Controls legacy enterprise domain) use department_requisitions, status=pending_approval and the /enterprise-inventory/approvals/execute endpoint; approval_rules entity_type=department_requisition governs that specific workflow.
The centralized Approvals UI built rows only from the second source and had no fetch for submitted inventory_requisitions. Therefore inventory requisitions were invisible even though Manager could handle them directly.

FRONTEND CORRECTION
Inventory Controls -> Approvals now displays 'Submitted inventory requisitions' in the existing Approval queue workspace using the SAME tenant-scoped protected GET /inventory-requisitions?status=submitted endpoint as the authoritative Requisitions page.
The query is gated by BOTH inventory_requisitions.read and inventory_requisitions.approve permissions. Only server-filtered submitted rows are shown. Pagination (20 displayed, fetch 21) prevents silently hiding records after an arbitrary single-page limit. The UI shows explicit loading, empty, and unavailable/error states with Retry, and refreshes on re-entry. Each row shows requisition number, requesting department, Submitted status, and Open requisition linking to the exact record through the existing requisitionId deep link.
The existing Department requisition, invoice, supplier return, and cycle-count queue/action paths remain untouched and visible separately beneath 'Other approval requests'. The previous generic empty message is restricted to those other requests so a populated inventory requisition list is not contradicted.
The UI explicitly explains these are separate workflows: Department requisition approval rules do NOT automatically govern inventory_requisitions. No false approval-rule linkage is claimed.
Added full English/German/Spanish/French/Croatian catalog entries for 9 new messages.

SAFETY / NON-CHANGE BOUNDARIES
No backend code, database, permissions, approval rule, status mutation, stock, fulfillment, audit or endpoint logic changed. No invented inventory requisition authorization via enterprise /approvals/execute; review and approve continue only through the Requisitions page, retaining version/threshold/self-approval safeguards. Existing tenant isolation remains enforced by GET /inventory-requisitions and its tenant-bound SQL. No attempt made to retroactively place fulfilled REQ-000003 into the live pending queue.

EXACT SOURCE DIFF VS 070
Frontend: three modified paths and one new regression guard, no deletions.
- package.json
- src/components/enterpriseInventory/tabs/ApprovalsTab.tsx
- src/i18n/tenantUiTranslations.ts

Backend: none; remains cumulative 070.

VERIFICATION
- New Batch 071 source/permission/tenant/deep-link/pagination/error/language regression: 31/31 PASS

confirmed: UsersPage uses PUT /users/:id, PATCH /users/:id/status with JSON
`is_active: boolean`; shared tenantMutationSuccessMessage only had generic
`User saved successfully.` fallback for PUT/PATCH user operations. All five
accurate user-success labels already have five-language translations.

IMPLEMENTATION — FRONTEND 078 ONLY
- In src/lib/api.ts, add strict boolean JSON field reader, returning null for
  missing, invalid or non-boolean values.
- For PATCH /users/:id/status, produce `User activated successfully.` when
  is_active===true or `User deactivated successfully.` when is_active===false.
  Invalid/missing body falls back safely to `User updated successfully.`
  without inventing an activation state.
- For PUT/PATCH /users/:id, show `User updated successfully.`.
- Keep POST /users creation and DELETE /users/:id deletion messages intact.
- Preserve the centralized toast dispatcher, translator, API payload/paths,
  backend validation, role/permission safety, query invalidation, and existing
  page-level status/errors. No new i18n strings or schema changes.
- Add executable isolated TypeScript logic regression:
  scripts/check-users-lifecycle-feedback-surgical-fix-v349316.mjs
- Register `npm run check:users-lifecycle-feedback-v349316` in package.json.
No Backend change. No migration. No other source component changed.

SURGICAL IMPLEMENTATION — FRONTEND 080 ONLY

     /users POST creation or /users/{id} PUT update, HTTP 409 and backend
     UNIQUE_CONSTRAINT_VIOLATION. If constraint metadata is supplied, it must
     match users_email_tenant_unique; unknown named constraints are NOT assumed
     to be duplicate email. Missing constraint detail is tolerated for legacy
     error responses; other endpoints, methods, and statuses remain unchanged.
   - Existing tenantMutationErrorMessage now maps only this confirmed case to
     the already-translated business wording. It suppresses the request ID
     only in the user-facing toast for this confirmed validation condition.

     are unchanged; no backend or data/migration change.

     negative routing/constraint/status and unchanged-boundary regression.

     narrow duplicate-email branch while still requiring normal update failure
     fallback and field-level errors. Prior 27 checks remain intact.
